b. Termination for Cause.  County may terminate this Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to perfo rm this Agreement; disregards laws, 
ordinances, rules, regulations or or ders of a public authority having 
jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a ma terial breach of this Agreement.  
When any of the foregoing causes exist, County shall give Consultant written 
notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains 
uncured, County may (without prejudice to any rights or remedies it may 
have) terminate this Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  
If the unpaid balance of the Agreement exceeds the cost of finishing the work 
(including expenses incurred by the County because of the Consultant’s 
default), Consultant shall be entitled to payment for its performance up to the

balance, Consultant shall pay the difference to County. 
 
c. Termination for Convenience.   County, by written notice to Consultant, 
may terminate this Agreement in whole or  part when in the sole discretion of 
County, it is in County’s best interests to  do so.  Consultant shall be paid for 
all material, equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable

provided that such payments, exclusiv e of termination expenses, shall not

previously made to Consu ltant.  Consultant shall not  be entitled to profit and 
overhead on material, equipment and services which were not provided. 
 
d. Consultant’s Responsibility.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, 
Consultant shall (a) prompt ly discontinue all servi ces (unless notice directs 
otherwise), and (b) deliver or otherwis e make available to County copies of 
all data, reports, summaries and other information and materials developed or 
accumulated by Consultant in performing this Agreement. 
 
15. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon 
written notice to County if performance is made impossible for a period of thirty 
(30) consecutive calendar days for any of  the following reasons through no act or 
fault of Consultant or its agents, employ ees or subcontractors:  issuance of an 
order by a count or other public aut hority having jurisdiction; an act of 
government, such as a declarat ion of national emergency; or a natural disaster or 
other Act of God.  Consultant may al so terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure if County fails to make any payment 
within time set forth in Section 6.  Cons ultant may also terminate this Agreement 
upon written notice if suspensions, delays or  interruptions by County equal in the 
aggregate more than 100% of the total number of days scheduled for completion.

email originated from outside of Navajo County. Please be careful and think before you click. 
HI Cheryl,  
 
Thank you for the opportunity to clarify our bid submission.  The questions that you posed are as stated: 
 
1. Is the sample invoice of our billing consistent with how Navajo County Bids will be priced using your provided sample 
for calculation purposes? 
 
The answer is yes. Our system
 bills in quarter inch increments.  
 
Since the legal measured at 7.52” in our system, it is rounded up to the nearest quarter inch to 7.75” which would be 
multiplied by 1.97 = $15.27 per publication  x 2% usage tax .42 = $15.58 x 2 = $31.16. 
 
 
 
I hope that answered your questions.  if not please don’t hesitate to contact me. 
 
 
I hope you have a great weekend.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2020  
Title: B20-02-014 PSPRS and CORP Boards - Legal Counsel
Submitted For: Lea Petersen, Deputy Commander 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Sheriff 
Presented By: Lea Peterson
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of contract B20-02-014 Local Public Safety Personnel Retirement System
(PSPRS) and Corrections Officers Retirement Plan Boards (CORP) - Legal Counsel to Ryan Rapp
& Underwood, PLC.
Background:
Navajo County entered into an agreement with Public Safety Personnel Retirement in 1979 and
the Corrections Officer Retirement in 1987.  As part of the agreement, we have a local PSPRS and
CORP Board.   When necessary, the Boards need to seek legal counsel for various items such as
personnel and employment matters, laws, legislation, etc.  The contract also includes that legal
counsel will provide training to our local board regarding applicable laws and procedures and
assist the PSPRS and CORP Boards in establishing and identifying training programs for the
PSRPS and CORP Boards.  This is a three year countract with the option to renew two additional
one-year terms.  The PSPRS and CORP Boards are outside of the jurisdiction of the Navajo
County Attorney’s Office and therefore they cannot provide legal counsel to the PSPRS or CORP
Boards.
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors office and publicly opened on May
13, 2020.  Six firms were notified; however, only one proposal was received.  The proposal was
evaluated per the criteria set forth in the Request for Qualifications.
Recommendation: 
The Evaluation Committee is recommending award of Contract B20-02-014 Local Public Safety
Personnel Retirement System (PSPRS) and Corrections Officers Retirement Plan Boards (CORP)
- Legal Counsel to Ryan Rapp & Underwood, PLC.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year:
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:

<!-- page 148 -->
County shall pay to Consultant a billing rate of $275.00 per hour for Partner’s and $210.00 per hour
for Associate’s. Payments shall be made within 30 days of County’s receipt of Consultant’s
monthly invoices

 CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES 
 
 
AGREEMENT made as of June 23, 2020 , Effective July 1, 2020 thru June 30, 2023 
between Navajo County ("County") and Ryan Rapp Underwood and Pacheco, PLC. 
 
WHEREAS, The Public Safety Personnel Retirement (PSPRS) and Corrections Officers Retirement Plan  
(CORP) Board has need of a law firm to serve as legal counsel for an initial three-year term with two one-
year renewal options. 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.   Consultant shall perform the scope of work described in the Request for 
Qualifications #B20-02-014 Local Public Safety Personnel Retirement  and 
Corrections Officers Retirement Plan Boards – Legal Counsel and submittal response 
dated April 13, 2020 , and those additional items set forth in the Consultant’s 
Compensation proposal dated April 13, 2020, in a competent and professional manner 
to the satisfaction of County.  The Scope of Work and the Consultant’s compensation 
proposal are attached hereto and by this reference incorporated herein.  If any 
incorporated term is incon sistent with the Agreement, this Agreement shall control.   
 
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, capacity 
and professional expertise to perform this Agreement.  Consultant shall provide an 
adequate staff of e xperienced personnel, capable of and devoted to the successful 
accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall assign 
specific individuals to key positions.  Once assigned to work under this Agreement, key 
personnel shall not be removed or replaced without County’s prior written approval.  
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the othe r.  An employee or agent of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or  agent of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be withheld from any payment by County, and Consultant 
shall be solely responsible for such matters.

<!-- page 150 -->
Page -2- 
4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulat ions and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed on 
Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 9, 
10, 12, 15 and 18.  Consultant is  responsible for full performance of this Agreement 
regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.   Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of car e and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing u nder similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care and 
the orderly progress of the Project, and in accordance with an agreed upon schedule for 
the performance of the Serv ices hereunder.  Time limits established by a schedule 
approved by County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by County or 
Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project proceeds.  The 
date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days that performance is 
delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other causes beyond 
Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the parties’ 
representatives. 
 
6. Payment.  County shall pay to Consultant a not-to-exceed billing rate of $275.00 per 
hour for Partner ’s and $210.00 per hour for A ssociate’s.  Payments shall be made 
within 30 days of County’s receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices.  Each invoice shall 
detail the work performed d uring the billing period.  Invoice amounts in percent of 
Projec t cost shall not exceed the percentage of completion of the Project as approved 
by County.   
 
7. Defects in Work.   County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifications.  Consultant agrees to promptly remedy 
all such de ficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal negotiation as set forth in Section 12.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any rejected work until such issues have been resolved. 
 
8. Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Consultant shall maintain in effect, at all times during the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect County and its agents, representativ es, 
officers, officials and employees against such losses as set for th below.  
Consultant shall provide County with a current Certificate of Insurance or a 
certified copy of the insurance policy naming County as an additional insured 
(except for Errors and Omissions / Professional Liability coverage).  
 
B. The following types and amounts of insurance are required as minimums: 
  
 
Worker's Compensation    Statutory

           and annual aggregate

Standard minimum deduct ibles are allowed.  Any deductibles are the 
responsibility of Consultant.  Consultant shall immediately inform  County of 
any cancellation of insurance or any decrease in the amount of coverage at least 
30 days before such action takes pl ace.  A violation of this provision may be 
treated as a material breach by County.  Consultant shall notify County upon any 
termination of its regular professional liability coverage and shall obtain tail 
coverage for a minimum of five years from the termination date. 
 
C. Consultant acknowledges that the amounts of insurance coverage stated above 
shall not limit its liability under this Agreement. 
 
9. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable by law, Consultant shall defend, indemnify 
and hold harmless County and its representatives, officers, officials and employees (the 
“Indemnitees”) from and against any and all claims, damages, losses and expenses 
(including but not limited to attorney fee s, court costs, and the costs of appellate 
proceedings) relatin g to, arising out of or resulting from Consultant’s negligent acts, 
errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this Agreement. Consultant’s duty 
to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise in connection with 
any claim, damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily injury, sickness, 
disease, death or injury to, impairment or destruction of property including loss of use 
resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent act, error, mistake or 
omission in  the performance of  this Agreement (including those by any person for 
whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may be liable)  to the 
extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of insurance coverage 
requirements set forth above shall not limit the scope of the indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
10. Records.  Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor to retain, all 
books, accounts, reports, files and other records relating to this Agreement for a period 
of five years af ter completion of the work.  All such documents shall be subject to 
inspection and audit by County upon reasonable notice during normal business hours.  A  
legible copy of any or all such documents shall be produced by Consultant at the request 
and expense of County. 
 
11. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement 
without County’s prior written approval, nor shall Consultant delegate an y duty 
hereunder without County’s prior written approval.

12. Negotiation of Di sputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any  
claim or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be pr ovided in writing, with supporting documentation, to the 
recipient designated in S ection 21.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes within a 
reasonable period of  not less than ten days, either party may pursue available lega l 
remedies. 
 
13. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
A. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made to 
the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting from 
the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the extent that 
performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or in terrupted by another 
cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for C ause.  County may terminate this Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards law s, 
ordinances, rules, regulations or orders of a public authority having jurisdiction; or 
otherwise commits a material breach of this Agreement.  When any of the foregoing 
causes exist, County shall give Consultant written notice and a ten-day opportunity 
to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, County may (without prejudice 
to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this Agreement and finish the 
work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of the Agreement exceeds the 
cost of finishing the work (including any expenses incurred by County as a result of 
Consultant’s default), Consultant shall be entitled to payment for its performance up 
to the amount of such excess.  If the cost of finishing the work exceeds the unpaid 
balance, Consultant shall pay the difference to County. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.   County, by written notice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County it is 
in County’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be pa id for all material , 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and a 
reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of te rmination expenses, shall not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any payments previously made to Consultant.  Consultant 
shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and services 
which were not provided. 
 
D. Consultant’s Responsibilities.   Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant 
shall (a) promptly discontinue all services (unless the notice directs otherwise), and 
(b) deliver or otherwise make available to County copies of all data, design 
calculations, drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries and oth er 
information and materials developed or accumulated by Consultant in performing 
this Agreement.

14. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to  County if performance is made impossible for a period of 30 co nsecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by a court or other public 
authority having jurisdiction; an act of government, such as a declar ation of national 
emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also terminate 
this Agreement upon written notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure if County fails to 
make any payment within the time set forth in Section 6.  Consultant may also terminate 
this Agreement upon written notice if suspensions, delays or interruptions by County 
equal in the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of days scheduled for 
completion. 
 
15. Governing Law.  This Agreement shall be governed by the law of the State of Arizona. 
 Any suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of Arizona 
(with venue in Navajo County) or the federal District of Arizona, but only after informal 
negotiation pursuant to Section 12. 
 
16. Incorporation of Appli cable Laws.   Every provision of law required by statute or 
regulation to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included herein.  
Each party shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such provision has 
been omitted. 
 
17. Non-Collusion.   The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 are incorporated herein by this 
reference.  
 
18. One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Consultant on any 
claim based upon or arising out of this Agreement unless such action is commenced 
within one year after County’s final payment hereunder. 
 
19. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement and those documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understandi ng between the parties.  No amendment shall be 
effective unless  executed by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.  
Provided, however, that the County’s project manager is authorized to modify the Scope 
of Work, in writing, with the concur rence of Consultant so long as the project is not 
changed substantially, or significant additional compensation is not required. 
 
20. Severability.  The provisions of this Agreement are severable.  Any provision held to 
be invalid or unenforceable shall not affect the validity or enforceability of any other 
provision. 
 
21.  Notices.   All notices, invoices and payments shall be in writing and may be given by 
personal delivery or certified mail.  The designated recipients are as follows:

Board of Supervisors Regular 9. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2020  
Title: Presentation - Local Workforce Development Board Updates
Submitted By: Stephanie Ray, Workforce Executive Director
Department: Northeastern Az Innovative Workforce
Presented By: Stephanie Ray
Subject:
Presentation - Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board Updates
Background: 
The presentation will cover the following updates:
*Review Goals and Strategies for 2020-2023 Strategic Plan
*Review of FY21 Budget
*Update on Projects being implemented by the Northeastern Arizona Community Resource, our
partner nonprofit organization
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors serve as the Chief Elected Official for the Northeastern
Arizona Local Workforce Development Area and work in partnership with the Local Workforce
Development Board to carry out the functions required by the Workforce Opportunity and
Innovation Act, including: 
*Developing and submiting a local workforce development business plan
*Developing an operating budget for carrying out the duties of the Workforce Development Board
*Selecting the One Stop Operator

Board of Supervisors Regular 10. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2020  
Title: BIA Task Agreement NO. 20 for Road Maintenance Equipment 
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of approval of Task Agreement NO. 20 with BIA for the purchase of one 2020
Caterpillar 416-07 Backhoe Loader from Empire Machinery in the amount of $108,110.13
utilizing District II Special Road Funds.
Background:
Over the years the County has developed a successful partnership with the Bureau of Indian
Affairs that features using Navajo County approved District Special Road funds to fund temporary
equipment operators hired by the County, with specific road work assigned by the BIA.  The
partnership is structured in such a way that Navajo County and the BIA each provide resources for
two motor graders (one in District I and one in District II) used on public tribal roads.  Navajo
County and BIA staff have identified the need for a backhoe to be used in road maintenance
activities on tribal roads in District II.  If approved, the backhoe will be owned by the County and
procured with District II Special Road funds.
    
The City of Tucson has a contract with Caterpillar, Inc. for the purchase and/or lease of heavy
equipment. Empire Machinery is a dealer for Caterpillar, Inc. and this contract is available for the
use of public and nonprofit agencies that have entered into a Cooperative Purchasing Agreement
with the City of Tucson's Department of Procurement or registered with the National IPA.
According to the City of Tucson's website, Navajo County is a Cooperative Partner, and we are
also registered with the National IPA. 
Approval of Task Agreement NO. 20 with the BIA provides useful equipment for helping to keep
Tribal roads and school bus routes passable in bad weather and throughout the year.
 
Recommendation: 
Approve Task Agreement NO. 20 with the BIA for purchase of road maintenance equipment.

Task Agreement NO. 20 
Fort Defiance Agency – Division of Transportation 
Navajo County Backhoe 
  
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board’s regular meeting on June 
23, 2020, has approved the expenditure of up to $108,110 in (X) Special Project Road Funds, or ( ) 
Highway User Revenue Funds  to purchase a backhoe used for road maintenance activities on BIA public 
roads, which are located on the Navajo Indian Reservation within BIA Chinle and Fort Defiance Agency, 
in Navajo County. The funds shall be used to purchase maintenance equipment in support of road 
maintenance activities on the BIA public roads in that portion of the Navajo Reservation, within Navajo 
County’s Supervisorial District No. 2. Such maintenance activities may include, but are not limited, to: 
installing road signs; mowing vegetation; repairing cattleguards and guardrails; cleaning, repairing, or 
installing drainage structures; repairing right-of-way fences; patching and crack sealing pavement, 
placing gravel to alleviate a safety hazard when directed by BIA supervisor; and performing similar road 
maintenance activities.  
The BIA warrants and represents that the equipment funded with the Allocated Funds will be used 
primarily for maintenance activities on roads within District 2 of Navajo County. BIA understands that 
Navajo County will be responsible for purchasing and maintaining the equipment. As described and 
mutually agreed to in previous task agreements, most recently Task Agreement #19 authorizing the 
hiring of temporary equipment operators, the BIA will provide maintenance activities whenever 
employe e(s) are working on BIA public roads. Navajo County will provide a backhoe (and the previously 
authorized motor grader) to be utilized while BIA will furnish any additional equipment needed for the 
employee(s). The County will be responsible for routine maintenance checkups on the backhoe it 
provides.

Consideration of approving the renewal Memorandum of Agreement with Bureau of Indian
Affairs-Gallup Region and Navajo County for partnership in road projects. 
Background:
For the past five years Navajo County and Bureau of Indian Affairs have had a very successful
partnership in which we assisted each other for the benefit of the Navajo Communities.This
agreement allowed us to provide material, manpower, equipment and other road related assistance
to BIA roads with the goal of providing safe and passable tribal roads within Navajo County. This
past year our five-year agreement expired so Navajo County Public Works would like to renew
this once again for another five years so the County can continue to assist BIA Department of
Transportation in maintaining Navajo Inventoried roads.

MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT BETWEEN  
BURE A U OF INDI AN AFFAI RS , NAVA JO REGI ON A L OFFI C E  
AND 
NAVAJO COUNTY, STATE OF ARIZONA  
 
 
THIS MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT ("MOA'') is entered into and between Navajo County, 
a political subdivision of the State Of Arizona (the "County"), and the Bureau of Indian Affairs, 
Navajo Regional Office, an agency of the United States Department of the Interior (the “BIA”)  
 
Article I  
Authority 
 
1.1 The county is authorized by A.R.S. 11-251 and 11- 952 to enter into intergovernmental agreements 
for cooperative action with agencies of the United States and/or Tribal governments. 
 
1.2 A.R.S. 11-251 (4) authorizes the county to maintain all public roads within its boundaries. 
 
1.3 A.R.S. 11-251 (29) authorizes the Board of Supervisors of the County to enter into 
agreements for the maintenance of highways, including those which pass through Indian 
lands, with the government of the United States, acting through its duly authorized officers 
or agents pursuant to any act of  Congress. 
 
1.4 The above statutes have been interpreted by the Arizona Attorney General in Opinion No. 
186-029 to authorize the expenditure of funds for the maintenance of all public roads within 
Indian country (as defined in 18  U.S.C. 1151). 
 
1.5 The code of Federal Regulations provides in 25 C.F.R. 170.6 that "The administration and 
maintenance of Indian Reservation Roads and Bridges is basically a function of the local 
Government Subject to availability of funds, the Commissioner (of Indian Affairs) shall 
maintain, or cause to be maintained, those approved roads on the Federal -Aid Indian Road  
System." 
 
1.6 25 C.F.R. 70.7 provides that “The Commissioner may enter into an agreement with the State 
(which is defined to include political subdivisions such as county) for cooperation in the 
maintenance of certain Indian Reservation Roads and Bridges, especially at those locations 
where road projects serve on non -Indian land as well as Indian  land. 
 
1.7 The BIA may retain all fu nding received pursuant to this MOA without depositing the 
same in the Miscellaneous  Receipts Account of the United States Treasury. The BIA has 
the authority to retain these  funds and use them for the road maintenance  that supplements  
but does not replace maintenance projects funded by the appropriate funds. Pursuant to 
the broad authorities of the Secretary of Interior over Indian Affairs, the regulations in 
25 C.F.R. Part 170, and the broad authorities in Title 23 U.S.C.A. (Federal Lands 
Highways Programs) governing Indian Reservation roads and intergovernmental 
cooperation,  the BIA and State/County  Cooperation  on road maintenance  of Indian

<!-- page 166 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
reservation roads is permitted , including BIA’s receipt of funding from the State or 
County for use in the maintenance of Indian Reservation roads.  
 
 
 
 
 
Article II  
Purpose 
 
2.1  Navajo County has agreed to provide funds to the BIA f or th e BIA’s road-related activities on 
the portion of the Navajo Reservation within the boundaries of Navajo County, Arizona. The 
B I A  affirms that it has the ability to perform such road-related activities and agrees to accept 
the funds granted by the County pursuant to this MOA. 
 
2.2  The parties intend for this MOA to serve as the master agreement between them for road -
related maintenance activities and the funding thereof, each such activity to be described in 
a Task agreement to the attached hereto. Task Agreements shall describe the effective date, 
term, amount of Allocated Funds, fund ing source(s), purpose(s), disbursement schedule and 
requirements, accounting  requirements, and such other terms and conditions as the parties may 
mutually agree upon, and shall be attached hereto upon approval by both parties. Each Task 
Agreement shall be subject to  the terms and conditions of the MOA. 
 
Article III 
Responsibilities 
 
3.1  The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board's regular meeting 
on the date shown in each Task Agreement, shall have approved the expenditure of specific 
funds (the "Allocated Funds") for specified road-related activities within that portion of the 
Navajo Reservation located within one or more Supervisorial Districts (this refers to the five 
districts into which the County is divided pursuant to the Arizona Revised Statues). 
 
3.2  The Allocated Funds shall be used by the BIA solely for the purpose(s) specified in each Task 
Agreement. 
 
3.3  The Allocated Funds are intended to supplement other monies that are or may become available 
to the BIA for road-related activities within that portion of the Navajo Nation located within 
Supervisorial District(s) indicated in each Task Agreement. The receipt of the Allocated Funds 
shall not cause other monies to be diverted or allocated to projects outside that portion of the 
Navajo Reservation within Supervisorial District(s) indicated in each Task Agreement. 
 
3.4  The Allocated Funds shall be disbursed to the BIA as shown in each Task Agreement and shall 
be used solely for the purpose(s) specified therein. The procedure for disbursing the Allocated 
Funds and accounting for their expenditure shall be mutually agreed upon by the respective 
financial officers of the parties and shall be indicated in each Task Agreement.

<!-- page 167 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
3.5  Any Allocated Funds that are unused by the date(s) or time frame(s) shown in each Task 
Agreement shall be refunded to the county within 30 days unless the Task Agreement has been 
extended or renewed by mutual written consent of the County and BIA. Upon the mutual 
written consent of the County and BIA, this MOA and/or each Task Agreement may be 
renewed for one or more additional  periods. 
 
3.6  The funding described in each Task Agreement is subject to the availability of funds to the 
County from the source(s) indicated in the Task Agreement. 
 
3.7  Each Party warrants that this MOA has been executed by the duly authorized official and that all 
requisite action has been taken for this MOA to be valid and binding obligation of the part y. 
 
Article IV 
Key Officials  
 
4.1 The key officials for each party are as follows: 
 
Glenn Kephart, County Manager Herby Larsen, Transportation Manager 
Navajo County BIA DOT-Navajo Region  
P. O. Box 668 301 W. Hill Ave 
Holbrook. AZ 86025 Gallup, NM 87301 
(928) 524-4000 (505) 863-8281 
 
Article V 
Reporting, Billing and Payme nt 
 
5.1 Records and Reports: Records and reports will be maintained by both the County and the BIA-
Navajo Region for all activities, funding and expenditures related to this MOA and each Task 
Agreement. Upon request by either party, the other party shall provide access to or copies of all 
such records and report  s. 
 
5.2 Funding: Disbursement of Allocated Funds to the BIA and accounting for expenditures by the 
BIA shall be made in accordance with each Task Agreement. 
 
Article VI 
Term of MOA  
 
6.1 This MOA becomes effective on the date of the last signature and  shall remain in force and 
effect for 5 years . Either party may propose amendments to this MOA or any Task 
Agreement by submitting  them in writing to the other party at least 30 days before the 
proposed implementation date.  Amendments must be approved in writing by both parties. 
 
6.2 In the event of any controversy which may arise out of this MOA or any Task Agreement, 
the County and BIA agree to select a procedure and forum mutually acceptable to them to 
informally resolve such dispute . 
 
6.3 Appropriation (Anti Deficiency Act. 31 U.S.C. 1341): Nothing contained in this Agreement

<!-- page 168 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
shall be construed as binding the BIA  to expend in any one fiscal year any sum in excess of 
appropriations made by Congress , as augmented by finding pursuant to this MOA, for the 
purpose of this Agreement for that fiscal y ear, or other obligation for the further expenditure 
of money in e xcess of such appropriations. 
 
6.4 Nothing in this MOA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to grant or alter any right-of-
way easement over roads on the Navajo Reservation or grant or alter the existing jurisdiction 
of any government over Indian reservation roads on the Navajo Reservation. 
 
6.5 Nothing in this MOA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to allocate or shift any current 
or future liability of a party to this MOA due to such party's acts or omissions to the other 
party to this MOA. 
 
6.6 Nothing in this MOA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to establish or enhance any 
rights by any person, government, or other entity not a party to this MOA. Nothing in this 
MOA shall prevent the BIA from consulting with representatives of Navajo County, Arizona, 
concerning the BIA’s road maintenance activities pursuant to this MOA. 
 
 
 
 
Article VII 
Authorized Signatures

Title: Memorandum of Agreement Navajo County and Kayenta Township
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of approval of Memorandum of Agreement and Task Agreement Number 1
between Navajo County and Kayenta Township formalizing partnership for transportation
projects.
Background:
Kayenta is the first municipally-structured government on the Navajo Nation and serves as a
permanent political subdivision/local government.  The Township is currently in design of the
U.S. 160 Streetlight Project in coordination with the Arizona Department of Transportation. 
During project planning, the Township identified the need for additional streetlights, the funding
for which they are responsible.  As indicated in the attached documentation, the Township is
pursuing formal agreements with ADOT for the purchase of approximately 50 streetlight poles at
an estimated cost of $113,600.  
 
The proposed MOA between Navajo County and the Kayenta Township will facilitate
partnerhship opportunities for transportation and road maintenance projects.  The proposed Task
Agreement 1 specifically identifies the Township's request for reimbursement funding for a
portion of the streetlight purchase in the amount of $60,000 from District I HURF funds.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of the MOA and Task Agreement 1.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2020
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $60,000
Fiscal Impact:
Adequate funding is available in Public Works HURF budget District 1 road funds.

KAYE N T A TOW NS HI P

THIS MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT ("MOA'') is entered into and between Navajo 
County, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona (the "County"), and the Kayenta 
Township (“the Township”), a political subdivision of the Navajo Nation  
 
Article I  
Authority 
 
1.1 The county is authorized by A.R.S. 11-251 and 11- 952 to enter into intergovernmental 
agreements for cooperative action with agencies of the United States and/or Tribal 
governments. 
. 
1.2 A.R.S. 11-251 (4) authorizes the county to maintain all public roads within its 
boundaries. 
 
1.3 A.R.S. 11-251 (29) authorizes the Board of Supervisors of the County to enter into 
agreements for the maintenance of highways, including those which pass through 
Indian lands, with the government of the United States, acting through its duly 
authorized officers o r agents pursuant to any act of  Congress. 
 
1.4 The above statutes have been interpreted by the Arizona Attorney General in Opinion 
No. 186-029 to authorize the expenditure of funds for the maintenance of all public 
roads within Indian country (as defined in 18  U.S.C. 1151). 
 
1.5 The code of Federal Regulations provides in 25 C.F.R. 170.6 that "The administration 
and maintenance of Indian Reservation Roads and Bridges is basically a function of 
the local Governmen t Subject to availability of funds, the Commissioner (of Indian 
Affairs) shall maintain, or cause to be maintained, those approved roads on the 
Federal-Aid Indian Road  System." 
 
1.6 25 C.F.R. 70.7 provides that “The Commissioner may enter into an agreement with 
the State (which is defined to include political subdivisions such as county) for 
cooperation in the maintenance of certain Indian Reservation Roads and Bridges, 
especially at those locations where road projects serve on non -Indian land as well as 
Indian land.

<!-- page 175 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
Article II  
Purpose 
 
2.1  Navajo County has agreed to provide funds to the Township f or th e ADOT road-related 
activities on the portion of the Navajo Reservation within the boundaries of Navajo 
County, Arizona. The T o w n shi p  affirms that it has the permission to perform such road-
related activities and agrees to accept the funds granted by the County pursuant to this 
MOA. 
 
2.2  The parties intend for this MOA to serve as the master agreement between them for 
road-related maintenance activities and the funding thereof, each such activity to be 
described in a Task agreement  to the attached hereto. Task Agreements shall describe 
the effective date, term, amount of Allocated Funds, fund ing source(s), purpose(s), 
disbursement schedule and requirements, accounting requirements, and such other 
terms and conditions as the parties may mutually agree upon, and shall be attached 
hereto upon approval by both parties. Each Task Agreement shall be subject to  the 
terms and conditions of the MOA. 
 
Article III 
Responsibilities 
 
3.1  The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board's regular 
meeting on the date shown in each Task Agreement, shall have approved the expenditure 
of specific funds (the "Allocated Funds") for specified road-related activities within that 
portion of the Navajo Reservation located within one or more Supervisorial Districts (this 
refers to the five districts into which the County is divided pursuant to the Arizona 
Revised Statues). 
 
3.2  The Allocated Funds shall be used by the Township solely for the purpose(s) specified in 
each Task Agreement. 
 
3.3  The Allocated Funds are intended to supplement other monies that are or may become 
available to the Kayenta Township for road-related activities within that portion of the 
Navajo Nation located within Supervisorial District(s) indicated in each Task Agreement. 
The receipt of the Allocated Funds shall not cause other monies to be diverted or 
allocated to projects outside that portion of the Navajo Reservation within Supervisorial 
District(s) indicated in each Task Agreement. 
 
3.4  The Allocated Funds shall be disbursed to the Township as shown in each Task 
Agreement and shall be used solely for the purpose(s) specified therein. The procedure 
for disbursing the Allocated Funds and accounting for their expenditure shall be mutually 
agreed upon by the respective financial officers of the parties and shall be indicated in 
each Task Agreement. 
 
3.5  Any Allocated Funds that are unused by the date(s) or time frame(s) shown in each Task 
Agreement shall be refunded to the county within 30 days unless the Task Agreement

<!-- page 176 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
has been extended or renewed by mutual written consent of the County and 
Township. Upon the mutual written consent  of the County and Township, this MOA 
and/or each Task Agreement may be renewed for one or more additional  periods. 
 
3.6  The funding described in each Task Agreement is subject to the availability of funds to 
the County from the source(s) indicated in the Task Agreement. 
 
3.7  Each Party warrants that this MOA has been executed by the duly authorized official 
and that all requisite action has been taken for this MOA to be valid and binding 
obligation of the part y. 
 
Article IV 
Key Officials  
 
4.1 The key officials for each party are as follows:

Article V 
Reporting, Billing and Payme nt 
 
5.1 Records and Reports: Records and reports will be maintained by both the County and the 
Kayenta Township for all activities, funding and expenditures related to this MOA and 
each Task Agreement. Upon request by either party, the other party shall provide access 
to or copies of all such records and report  s. 
 
5.2 Funding: Disbursement of Allocated Funds to the Township and accounting for 
expenditures by the BIA shall be made in accordance with each Task Agreement. 
 
Article VI 
Term of MOA  
 
6.1 This MOA becomes effective on the date of the last signature and  shall remain in force 
and effect for 2 year. Either party may propose amendments to this MOA or any Task 
Agreement by submitting  them in writing to the other party at least 30 days before the 
proposed implementation date.  Amendments must be approved in writing by both 
parties. 
 
6.2 In the event of any controversy which may arise out of this MOA or any Task 
Agreement, the County and Township agree to select a procedure and forum 
mutually acceptable to them to informally resolve such dispute . 
 
6.3 Appropriation (Anti Deficiency Act. 31 U.S.C. 1341): Nothing contained in this

year any sum in excess of appropriations made by Congress , as augmented by

appropriations. 
 
6.4 Nothing in this MOA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to grant or alter any 
right-of-way easement  over roads on the Navajo Reservation or grant or alter the 
existing jurisdiction of any government over State right of way within the Township.  
 
6.5 Nothing in this MOA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to allocate or shift any

6.6 Nothing in this MOA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to establish or enhance

Kayenta Township 
 
 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board’s regular 
meeting on June 23, 2020, has approved the expenditure of up to $20,000 in ( ) Special 
Project Road Funds, ( ) TEA 21 Funds, or (X) Highway User Revenue Funds (“Allocated 
Funds”) to be used to reimburse the purchase of street light poles that will be installed 
on US 160 within the Kayenta Township, in Navajo County District No. 1.The Allocated 
Funds shall be used to supplement other monies that are or may become available to 
the Township for road maintenance purposes within Kayenta Township boundary. The 
Township understands and acknowledges that the Allocated Funds are monies that the 
County has received from the Arizona Highway User Revenue Funds (HURF) and 
which are subject to audit by the Arizona Auditor General.  
 
The Township warrants and represents that the street light poles purchased/reimbursed 
will be funded with the Allocated Funds where it will be used primarily for maintenance 
activities on roads within District 1, which are open to the public and are eligible for the 
expenditure of HURF monies under Arizona Constitution and related Arizona Revised 
Statutes. The Kayenta Township agrees to fully cooperate in any audit of the Allocated 
Funds by the Arizona Auditor General.  
 
The receipt of the Allocated Funds shall not cause other monies to be diverted from 
projects within this area or allocated to projects outside this area.  
 
The County shall have no responsibility or liability whatsoever associated with 
purchase, installation, or performance of the streetlight poles. The Township shall be 
responsible for prioritizing, scheduling, and supervising all maintenance activities for 
which the Allocated Funds are used in whole or in part. It is understood that the 
County’s sole obligation hereunder is to provide a one-time maintenance service with 
the allocated funds.  
 
Any Allocated Funds that are unused as of December 30, 2020, shall no longer be 
available for distribution pursuant to this Task Agreement.

sent to:

RESOLUTION OF THE KAYENTA TOWNSHIP COMMISSION 
 
Approving and Authorizing the Purchase of ADOT Street Light Poles for the ADOT U.S. 
Highway 160 Street Light Project 
 
WHEREAS: 
 
1. The Kayenta Township Commission ("Commission") is a home -rule municipality of  the 
Navajo Nation with the general authority and responsibility to govern for the welfare of 
the Kayenta Township ("Township") and its residents, including the enactm ent of such 
ordinances, rules and regulations as it deems in the best in terest of the Township, see 2 
N.N.C. §§ 4081-4086 (2005); and  
2. The Township is currently in design of the U.S. 160 Street Light Project which is scheduled 
for construction in this Fiscal Year 2020; and 
3. To accelerate the project schedule the Township would like to procure the street light poles 
approved by ADOT and which has a 14-16 week lead time; and 
4. Per procurement policies the Township has requested for three quotes and has received 
quotes from Clark Tra nsportation Solutions, Lyle Northern Electric  and Ameron Pole 
Products attached hereto as Composite Exhibit “A”; and 
5. The Quote from Clark Transportation Solutions for ADOT Type G Light Pole s is the 
lowest quote in the amount of $113,6 00.00 and has been recommended by management; 
and 
6. Having reviewed the attached quotes and having received the recommendation of the 
Town Manager, the Commission now desires to approve the purchase of the ADOT Type 
G Light Poles, contingent upon review by the Town Manager and legal counsel of the 
purchase agreement, warranty information and any other associated documents, and 
successful negotiation of any terms in such documents as the Town Manager on advice of 
legal counsel deems prudent. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 
1. The Kayenta Township Commission hereby approves the purchase of the ADOT Type G 
Light Poles from Clark Transportation Solutions, contingent upon review by the Town 
Manager and legal counsel of the purchase agreement, warranty information and any 
other associated documents, and successful negotiation of any terms in such documents 
as the Town Manager on advice of legal counsel deems prudent. 
 
2. The Town Manager is hereby authorized to take any and all actions necessary and 
prudent to carry out the intent of this resolution.

Goods not paid for and make them available to Seller at a place to be designated by Seller. Any costs incurred by Seller as result of suspending or cancelling performance or repossession or
collection shall be payable by Buyer. Seller may, at its sole option, sell repossessed Goods, with the proceeds to be applied to the unpaid balance of Seller and expenses incurred by Seller in
sales, repossession and collection in any order of application determined to be appropriate by Seller at its sole discretion. Buyer shall pay any remaining deficiency. Subject to applicable law,
Seller shall have the right at any time to examine and take possession of such Goods without restrictive action by Buyer. Seller may, at its sole option, resume performance of this Contract upon
Buyer's performance of its obligations; Buyer's payment of all accrued amounts due to Seller; Buyer's removal of any inhibiting cause; and Buyer's providing sufficient assurance or security for its
performance as Seller, in its sole judgment, may determine to be required.
9. WAIVER: Neither the failure of Seller in any one or more instances to enforce one or more of the terms or conditions of this Contract or to exercise any contractual right or privilege, nor the
waiver by Seller of any breach of this Contract, shall be construed as subsequently waiving any terms, conditions, rights or privileges of this Contract, and the same shall continue and remain in
force and effect as if no such failure had occurred.
10. FORCE MAJEURE: Seller shall not be liable for non-performance or delay in performance resulting from any governmental law or regulation now or subsequently in effect or for delays
caused by suppliers of Seller, Acts of God, fire, flood, wind, sabotage, strikes (or other labor troubles), accidents, necessary repairs to machinery, adverse weather conditions, acts or omissions
of Buyer, or any other cause beyond Seller's reasonable control. In the event of any such event, Seller shall have the right to allocate and reschedule production and delivery of the Goods and/or
Services to Buyer as Seller, in its sole discretion, shall deem fair and practical and without any liability to Seller.
11. DELIVERY: All sales are FCA point of shipment, and title and risk of loss shall pass to Buyer upon delivery of the Goods to carrier, unless expressly stated otherwise in writing in this
Contract. If the FCA point is expressly stated in this Contract to be either destination or job site, title and risk of loss shall pass to Buyer upon arrival of the carrier at the destination or job site
boundary. If the FCA point is at the job site, unloading is Buyer's sole responsibility. Delivery to jobsite means motor truck delivery as close to jobsite as, in Seller's sole judgment, is safe and
practical for loaded truck and trailers operating under their own power. Seller may refuse delivery of Goods or the performance of any Services if the worksite conditions are, in Seller's sole
opinion, unsafe and/or do not meet the specifications required to perform the Services. In the event the Goods arrive to the job site and delivery is unable to be made, Buyer will incur additional
charges associated with the delivery and re-delivery of the Goods. Buyer shall provide and maintain at its expense suitable right of way, including access roads and turn arounds, to the jobsite.
Buyer shall not "snake" or pull any trucks with any power equipment without the express agreement of Seller and the delivering carrier. Standby time will be charged when in excess of one (1)
hour from the time of arrival of trucks at the jobsite when such delay is caused by Buyer's failure to comply with these provisions. Seller will make reasonable efforts to deliver Goods at the time
requested by Buyer, and agreed to by Seller. Delivery cannot be scheduled on less than forty-eight (48) hour written notice to Seller.
12. DELAY, TERMINATION OR CANCELLATION BY BUYER: Seller shall be entitled to recover all costs, expenses (including overhead costs and expenses) and damages incurred or suffered
as a result of Buyer's cancellation or termination of this Contract or as a result of Buyer's or Buyer's customer's postponement or other delay of Seller's performance under this Contract.
13. PATENTS: If the Goods covered by this Contract are manufactured pursuant to designs or plans provided by Buyer, Buyer shall indemnify and save harmless Seller, its agents and
subcontractors from any expense, cost, loss, damage or liability for infringement of any patents with respect to such Goods and their process of manufacture, and Buyer agrees at its own
expense to defend or assist, at Seller's sole option, in the defense of any action in which such infringement is alleged with respect to the manufacture, sale(s) or use of such Goods.
14. ASSISTANCE BY SELLER: No recommendation, statement or assistance given by Seller, its representatives or agents, in connection with the installation, application, storage, transportation
or use of the Goods furnished under this Contract shall constitute a waiver by Seller of any of its provisions, enlarge Seller's liability as defined and limited above, or be deemed to provide any
warranty in excess of Seller's warranties set in forth above.
15. ATTORNEY FEES & EXPENSES: If Seller must file suit or take other legal action to enforce any of its rights under this Contract (including proceedings to collect amounts due Seller per this
Contract), Buyer shall pay the reasonable attorney's fees and expenses incurred by Seller.
16. SALES: All sales are final. Any return of Goods shall be subject to the prior written approval of Seller. Goods returned for credit shall be returned at the sole expense of Buyer. Buyer shall pay
a minimum restocking charge of thirty five percent (35%) of the sales price for standard Goods and one hundred percent (100%) for non-standard Goods, plus any charges necessary to rework
the Goods to resalable condition.
17. SPECIFICATIONS, APPLICATION & USE OF GOODS: Goods furnished under this Contract are sold pursuant to Seller's manufacturing drawings and specifications and are subject to
standard manufacturing variations. Seller may change such drawings and time specifications at any time without Incurring liability for Goods previously or subsequently sold. Buyer shall be
responsible for the performance of Goods not produced to Buyer's specifications, drawings or plans. Buyer shall be responsible for determining the appropriate use or application of Seller's
goods for Buyer's or Buyer's customer's requirements, regardless of Buyer's solicitation of advice or recommendations from Seller or their provision by Seller. Buyer waives any claim against
Seller, its agents or employees arising out of Buyer's selection, application or use of the Goods furnished under this Contract. Buyer shall indemnify and defend Seller from any claim or suit for
personal injury (including death), property damage or other liability arising out of the improper selection or application, or any misuse, of Seller's Goods or the failure to follow Seller's application,
installation or safety Instructions or any applicable industry standards. Buyer shall be responsible for all wiring, foundation and design of foundations.
18. INSURANCE: Upon written request, each party shall furnish to the other party certificates of insurance evidencing the fact that the adequate insurance to support each party's obligations
hereunder has been secured. To the extent of each party's release and indemnity obligations expressly assumed by each party hereunder, each party agrees that all such insurance policies
shall, (a) be primary to the other party's insurance; (b) include the other party, its parent, subsidiary and affiliated or related companies, and its and their respective officers, directors, employees,
consultants and agents as additional insured; and, (c) be endorsed to waive subrogation against the other party, its parent, subsidiary and affiliated or related companies, and its and their
respective officers, directors, employees, consultants and agents.
19. REGULATORY COMPLIANCE: By acceptance of delivery under this Contract, Buyer warrants it has complied with all applicable governmental, statutory and regulatory requirements and will
furnish Seller with such documents as may be required. Seller warrants and certifies that in the performance of this Contract, it will comply with all applicable statutes, rules, regulations and
orders, including laws and regulations pertaining to labor, wages, hours and other conditions of employment, and applicable price ceilings if any. Seller will not provide any certification or other
documentation nor agree to any contract provision or otherwise act in any manner which may cause Seller to be in violation of applicable United States law, including but not limited to the Export
Administration Act of 1979 and regulations issued pursuant thereto. All Orders shall be conditional upon granting of export licenses or import permits which may be required. Buyer shall obtain
at its own risk any required export license and import permits and Buyer shall remain liable to accept and pay for material if licenses are not granted or are revoked.
20. INTERPRETATION: The laws of the State of Seller's location, as set forth in Seller's address on the masthead of this document, shall govern the interpretation and enforcement of this
contract, excluding only the law of conflicts. If security interest filings are made by Seller pursuant to the PAYMENT paragraph above, the Uniform Commercial Code as enacted in the State
wherein the filings are made shall govern that security interest and those filings.
21. FINAL AGREEMENT: There are no understandings or agreements of Buyer and Seller, or representations of either party, relating to this Contract that are not fully expressed in this Contract,
and no change shall be made to this Contract unless in writing and signed by duly authorized officers of Seller and Buyer. No part of Buyer's contract(s) with Buyer's customer is included in this
Contract, except as specifically and expressly incorporated in writing by Seller in this document. Buyer may not assign or otherwise delegate Buyer's obligations under this offer and any resulting
contract without Seller's express prior written consent.

Navajo County and Hopi Department of Transportation has always had a solid foundation of
partnership in regard to assisting with maintaining Hopi inventoried roads. We have had our
successful stories in which the County has assisted Hopi Tribe to maintain roads and providing

Renewal of the MOA for an additional five years continues the succesful partnership Navajo
County and Hopi DOT have enjoyed over the years.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT BETWEEN  
HOPI TRIBE DEPARTMENT OF TRANSPORTATION 
AND 
NAVAJO COUNTY, STATE OF ARIZONA  
 
 
THIS MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT ("MOA'') is entered into and between Navajo County, 
a political subdivision of the State Of Arizona (the "County"), and the Hopi Tribe. 
 
Article I  
Authority 
 
1.1 The county is authorized by A.R.S. 11-251 and 11- 952 to enter into intergovernmental agreements 
for cooperative action with agencies of the United States and/or Tribal governments. 
 
1.2 A.R.S. 11-251 (4) authorizes the county to maintain all public roads within its boundaries. 
 
1.3 A.R.S. 11-251 (29) authorizes the Board of Supervisors of the County to enter into 
agreements for the maintenance of highways, including those which pass through Indian 
lands, with the government of the United States, acting through its duly authorized officers 
or agents pursuant to any act of  Congress. 
 
1.4 The above statutes have been interpreted by the Arizona Attorney General in Opinion No. 
186-029 to authorize the expenditure of funds for the maintenance of all public roads within 
Indian country (as defined in 18  U.S.C. 1151). 
 
1.5 The code of Federal Regulations provides in 25 C.F.R. 170.6 that "The administration and 
maintenance of Indian Reservation Roads and Bridges is basically a function of the local 
Government Subject to availability of funds, the Commissioner (of Indian Affairs) shall 
maintain, or cause to be maintained, those approved roads on the Federal -Aid Indian Road  
System." 
 
1.6 25 C.F.R. 70.7 provides that “The Tribes may enter into an agreement with the State (which 
is defined to include political subdivisions such as county) for cooperation in the 
maintenance of certain Indian Reservation Roads and Bridges, especially at those locations 
where road projects serve on non -Indian land as well as Indian  land. 
 
1.7 The Hopi Tribe  may retain all funding received pursuant to this MOA without 
depositing the same in the Miscellaneous  Receipts Account of the United States Treasury. 
The Hopi Tribe  has the authority to retain these  funds and use them for the road 
maintenance  that supplements  but does not replace maintenance projects funded by the 
appropriate funds. Pursuant to the broad authorities of the Secretary of Interior over 
Indian Affairs, the regulations in 25 C.F.R. Part 170, and the broad authorities in Title 23 
U.S.C.A. (Federal Lands Highways Programs) governing Indian Reservation roads and 
intergovernmental cooperation,  the Hopi Tribe  and State/County  Cooperation  on road 
maintenance  of Indian reservation roads is permitted .

<!-- page 192 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
 
 
 
Article II  
Purpose 
 
2.1  Navajo County has agreed to provide funds to the Hopi  Tri be f or th eir Department of  
Transportation’s road-related activities on the portion of the Hopi Reservation within the 
boundaries of Navajo County, Arizona. The H o p i  T r i be affirms that it has the ability to perform 
such road-related activities and agrees to accept the funds granted by the County pursuant to 
this MOA. 
 
2.2  The parties intend for this MOA to serve as the master agreement between them for road -
related maintenance activities and the funding thereof, each such activity to be described in 
a Task agreement to the attached hereto. Task Agreements shall describe the effective date, 
term, amount of Allocated Funds, fund ing source(s), purpose(s), disbursement schedule and 
requirements, accounting requirements, and such other terms and conditions as the parties may 
mutually agree upon, and shall be attached hereto upon approval by both parties. Each Task 
Agreement shall be subject to  the terms and conditions of the MOA. 
 
Article III 
Responsibilities 
 
3.1  The Navajo County Board of Supervisors, by motion duly passed at the Board's regular meeting 
on the date shown in each Task Agreement, shall have approved the expenditure of specific 
funds (the "Allocated Funds") for specified road-related activities within that portion of the Hopi 
Reservation located within one or more Supervisorial Districts (this refers to the five districts 
into which the County is divided pursuant to the Arizona Revised Statues). 
 
3.2  The Allocated Funds shall be used by the Hopi Tribe solely for the purpose(s) specified in each 
Task Agreement. 
 
3.3  The Allocated Funds are intended to supplement other monies that are or may become available 
to the Hopi Tribe for road-related activities within that portion of the Hopi Reservation located 
within Supervisorial District(s) indicated in each Task Agreement. The receipt of the Allocated 
Funds shall not cause other monies to be diverted or allocated to projects outside that portion of 
the Hopi Reservation within Supervisorial District(s) indicated in each Task Agreement. 
 
3.4  The Allocated Funds shall be disbursed to the Hopi Tribe as shown in each Task Agreement and 
shall be used solely for the purpose(s) specified therein. The procedure for disbursing the 
Allocated Funds and accounting for their expenditure shall be mutually agreed upon by the 
respective financial officers of the parties and shall be indicated in each Task Agreement. 
 
3.5  Any Allocated Funds that are unused by the date(s) or time frame(s) shown in each Task

<!-- page 193 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
Agreement shall be refunded to the county within 30 days unless the Task Agreement has been 
extended or renewed by mutual written consent of the County and Hopi Tribe. Upon the 
mutual written consent  of the County and Hopi Tribe, this MOA and/or each Task Agreement 
may be renewed for one or more additional  periods. 
 
3.6  The funding described in each Task Agreement is subject to the availability of funds to the 
County from the source(s) indicated in the Task Agreement. 
 
3.7  Each Party warrants that this MOA has been executed by the duly authorized official and that all 
requisite action has been taken for this MOA to be valid and binding obligation of the part y. 
 
Article IV 
Key Officials  
 
4.1 The key officials for each party are as follows: 
 
Glenn Kephart, County Manager Michael Lomayaktewa, DOT Director 
Navajo County Hopi Department of Transportation 
P. O. Box 668 P.O. Box 123 
Holbrook. AZ 86025 Kykotsmovi, AZ 86038 
(928) 524-4000 (928)734-3182  
 
Article V 
Reporting, Billing and Payme nt 
 
5.1 Records and Reports: Records and reports will be maintained by both the County and the Hopi 
DOT for all activities, funding and expenditures related to this MOA and each Task Agreement. 
Upon request by either party, the other party shall provide access to or copies of all such 
records and report  s. 
 
5.2 Funding: Disbursement of Allocated Funds to the Hopi Tribe and accounting for expenditures by 
the Hopi Tribe shall be made in accordance with each Task Agreement. 
 
Article VI 
Term of MOA  
 
6.1 This MOA becomes effective on the date of the last signature and  shall remain in force and 
effect for 5 years . Either party may propose amendments to this MOA or any Task 
Agreement by submitting  them in writing to the other party at least 30 days before the 
proposed implementation date.  Amendments must be approved in writing by both parties. 
 
6.2 In the event of any controversy which may arise out of this MOA or any Task Agreement, 
the County and  the Hopi Tribe agree to select a procedure and forum mutually acceptable to 
them to informally resolve such dispute . 
 
6.3 Appropriation (Anti Deficiency Act. 31 U.S.C. 1341): Nothing contained in this Agreement 
shall be construed as binding the Hopi Tribe to expend in any one fiscal year any sum in

<!-- page 194 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
excess of appropriations made by Congress , as augmented by finding pursuant to this MOA, 
for the purpose of this Agreement for that fiscal y ear, or other obligation for the further 
expenditure of money in e xcess of such appropriations. 
 
6.4 Nothing in this MOA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to grant or alter any right-of-
way easement over roads on the Hopi Reservation or grant or alter the existing jurisdiction of 
any government over Indian reservation roads on the Hopi Reservation. 
 
6.5 Nothing in this MOA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to allocate or shift any current 
or future liability of a party to this MOA due to such party's acts or omissions to the other 
party to this MOA. 
 
6.6 Nothing in this MOA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to establish or enhance any 
rights by any person, government, or other entity not a party to this MOA. Nothing in this 
MOA shall prevent the Hopi Tribe from consulting with representatives of Navajo County, 
Arizona, concerning the Hopi Tribe’s road maintenance activities pursuant to this MOA. 
 
 
 
 
Article VII

Board of Supervisors Regular 10. e.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2020  
Title: BOS Direction Regarding Potential Nuisance Abatement of 197 Elk Drive
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of potential nuisance abatement of 197 Elk Drive - APN 311-07-023A.
Background:
The property at 197 Elk Drive has been inspected and cited for nuisance code violations for at
least seven years for unsightly and unsanitary or hazardous conditions.  The following is a
summary of the violation history and attempts by the County to notify the property owner to bring
the property into compliance: 
August 2013 – Notice of Inspection
February 2015 – Notice of Inspection
August 2016 – Notice of Inspection
August 2016 – County Attorney Office letter sent - Notification of potential legal action 
June 2017 – Notice of Inspection
December 2018 – Staff discussion with the property owner - Property owner requested 90
days to comply – did not comply
August 2019 – County Attorney Office letter – 2nd letter notifying property owner of
potential legal action
September 2019 – Property re-inspected - No improvement, remains non-compliant 
April 2020 – Public Works Director certified letter - Notification of intent to begin legal
abatement process and advisement of appeal rights and process.
The Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, Article 24 - Nuisance Abatement - specifies the
process to be used to clean up properties when multiple attempts to require the property
owner to do so have failed. Since our records indicate that we have received multiple
complaints from mulitiple residents over the past seven years, and since the property
remains in significant non-compliance to date, County staff has discussed this situation at
length and determined that following the abatement process is in the best interest of the

<!-- page 196 -->
County. Staff will present the issues for BOS consideration at the June 23rd meeting and
seek direction as to how to proceed. 
 
 
Recommendation: 
Authorize staff to proceed in the abatement process for 197 Elk Drive.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY20-21
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 15,000

Meeting Date: 06/23/2020  
Title: Hashknife Energy Center - District III - Request Approval of Special Use Permit
Amendment
Submitted By: Cody Cooper, Planner I
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Cody Cooper
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 20-2020
approving/denying a Special Use Permit Amendment to expand the site area for Hashknife
Energy Center from the previously approved 3,000 acres to approximately 3,960 acres, an
addition of 960 acres, to build and operate a 400 MW solar generation and storage facility on
APN 107-01-023, 107-05-031C, 107-05-031D, 110-15-001D, & 110-15-008, south of Joseph
City in Township 18 North, Range 19 East, Sections 30, 31, & 32, and Township 17 North, Range
19 East, Sections 05 & 06.
Background:
On November 12, a Special Use Permit was approved for the Hashknife Solar Project, subject to
conditions.  The original project was approved based on the site plan and narrative provided for
an area of approximately 3,000 acres.  The applicant has requested the ability to add a section and
a half to their site at this time (960 acres). 
 
Due to such a large addition of land to the site plan, the request made requires hearings to amend
the previously approved Special Use Permit.  The request being heard is to amend the previously
approved site area of 3,000 acres to approximately 3,960 acres, per the updated site plan. 
 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of the Special Use Permit Amendment, subject to the conditions
stated within the Staff Report.
