NAVAJO N  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: June 23, 2020 
To:   Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
From:   Cody Cooper, Planner I 
Subject: Public Hearing and consideration of Case Number SUP-A 20-001, a request for 
a Special Use Permit Amendment to expand the site are from the previously 
approved 3,000 acres to approximately 3,960 acres, an addition of 960 acres, to 
build and operate a 400 MW solar generation and storage facility. 
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Special Use Permit subject to the conditions of 
the attached resolution for SUP-A 20-001. 
 
Location: The property is located to the south of Joseph City.  The properties for this case are also 
known as APN 107-01-023, 107-05-031C, 107-05-031D, 110-15-001D, and 110-15-008. 
 
Size: Five (5) parcels totaling approximately 3,960 acres. 
 
Supervisor District: III 
 
Zoning: RU-20 (Rural Zoning District, 20 Acres Per Dwelling Unit) 
 
Property Owners: State of Arizona, Aztec East Jeffers LLC, Aztec Land Company LLC 
 
Applicant: Hashknife Energy Center LLC 
 
Representative: James Williams 
 
BACKGROUND/HISTORY: 
The original Special Use Permit for this case was heard on October 17, 2019 in front of the Navajo 
County Planning and Zoning Commission, where the item was recommended for approval to the 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of 3-1, subject to conditions. 
 
The second hearing for this Special Use Permit was held in front of the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors on November 12, 2019, where it was approved by a vote of 5-0, subject to the conditions 
of Resolution # 28-19 (attached).

<!-- page 211 -->
REQUEST DESCRIPTION: 
 
On November 12, a Special Use Permit was approved for the Hashknife Solar Project, subject to 
conditions.  The original project was approved based on the site plan and narrative provided for an 
area of approximately 3,000 acres.  The applicant has requested the ability to add a section and a half 
to their site at this time (960 acres).   
 
Due to such a large addition of land to the site plan, the request made requires hearings to amend the 
previously approved Special Use Permit.  The request being heard is to amend the previously 
approved site area of 3,000 acres to approximately 3,960 acres, per the updated site plan. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Article 29. 
 
This item has been noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 29 of 
the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
This proposal complies with the Comprehensive Plan and the Aztec Area Plan in the same manner as 
the originally approved Special Use Permit. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the 
property is located. 
 
The proposed use is not allowed by-right in the zoning district in which the property is located, 
which is why a Special Use Permit was previously required and an Amendment is now required. 
 
4. The proposed use is compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The proposed use is compatible with current and likely future uses of properties within its vicinity.  
The property is surrounded by mostly vacant grazing land and is not far from Cholla Power Plant and 
other land that is desirable for usage for solar power generation and storage. 
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
Given the rural nature of the property and current makeup of the nearby landscape, there should be 
minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare of properties in the vicinity 
of the proposed use. 
 
 
PLANNING AND ZONING STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Planning and Zoning Staff recommends approval of SUP-A 20-001, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. All improvements and infrastructure, including, but not limited to, solar arrays, inverters, signs, 
temporary construction offices, storage facilities, etc., shall adhere to all applicable Navajo 
County Codes, Standards, and Ordinances and are subject to a pplicable building permit and/or 
other permitting processes.  No building or structure may be constructed or occupied prior to

<!-- page 212 -->
complete compliance with all appropriate Public Works Department requirements.  This shall 
include, but not be limited to, grading and drainage plans, flood control requirements as deemed 
necessary, and the issuance of building, construction and/or other permits for all proposed 
improvements and structures. 
 
2. In the applicant’s building permit application for the limited construction  activities to occur 
within 2019, the applicant shall provide a statement addressing plans for transportation and 
access, construction management (including traffic control, site access, and lighting) and 
construction mitigation.  In addition, the applican t shall submit a best management practices 
plan to Navajo County for approval, to mitigate ground disturbed and limit impact to existing 
vegetation during the limited construction activities within 2019 only. 
 
3. Prior to the issuance of any building or relat ed permits and the commencement of construction 
occurring after 2019, the following are to be submitted for review and approval by the Navajo 
County Public Works Department. 
 
a. A Transportation and Access Plan/Statement. The applicant shall submit a 
Transportation and Access Plan/Statement for the development of the Hashknife Solar 
Project to the Navajo County Public Works Department for review and approval.  The 
Transportation Plan shall, at a minimum, address the following: 
 
i. Site access.  Provide specific and detailed information regarding off and on -site 
roadways, including roadway widths and surfacing.  Also, if deemed necessary, 
provide additional information regarding existing on -site roadways/easements, 
including title reports, other documentation regar ding the roadway(s), exhibits, 
and/or legal descriptions indicating the existing and proposed alignments.  Staff 
notes, and the applicant acknowledges, that the realignment, abandonment and/or 
dedication of roadways may require separate action by the Navajo County Board 
of Supervisors. 
 
b. Construction Management Plan.  The Applicant shall prepare a Construction 
Management Plan for the Solar Facility.  The plan shall address the following: 
 
i. Traffic control methods (in coordination with the Department of Transp ortation 
prior to initiation of construction), including lane closures, signage, and flagging 
procedures. 
 
ii. Site access planning directing employee and delivery traffic to minimize conflicts 
with local traffic. 
 
iii. Lighting. During construction of the Solar Facility, any temporary construction 
lighting shall be positioned downward, inward, and shielded to eliminate glare 
from all adjacent properties.  Emergency and safety lighting shall be exempt from 
this condition. 
 
c. Construction Mitigation Plan. The applicant shall prepare a Construction Mitigation Plan 
for the Solar Facility.  This plan shall address, at a minimum, the effective mitigation of 
dust, hours of construction activity, access and road improvements, and handling of 
general construction complaints. 
 
d. Revegetation/Restoration Plan.  A revegetation and restoration plan shall be prepared 
per Arizona Department of Environmental Quality (ADEQ) Storm Water Pollution 
Prevention Plan and Stormwater Permit requirements.  The applicant will consult with

<!-- page 213 -->
the landowners and National Resource Conservation Service (NRCS) as to the 
composition of the seed mix.  The revegetation and restoration plan must address any 
areas which may be disturbed during the construction and/or operation of the facility.  
This plan must also address the areas underneath the solar arrays. 
 
e. Grading and Drainage Plan.  The Solar Facility shall be constructed in compliance with 
the County -approved grading plan as determined and approved by the Planning and 
Zoning Director or their desi gnee prior to the commencement of any construction 
activities.  The grading plan shall: 
 
i. Clearly show existing and proposed contours 
 
ii. Note the locations and amount of soil to be removed, if any, and the percent of 
the site to be graded 
 
iii. Limit grading to th e greatest extent practicable by avoiding steep slopes and 
laying out arrays parallel to landforms 
 
iv. Take advantage of natural flow patterns in drainage design and keep the amount 
of impervious surface as low as possible to reduce stormwater storage needs. 
 
f. Wildlife Corridors.  The applicant shall identify an access corridor for wildlife to 
navigate through the Solar Facility.  The proposed wildlife corridor shall be shown on 
the site plan submitted to the County.  Areas between fencing shall be kept open to allow 
for the movement of migratory animals and other wildlife.  Width of corridor to be 
determined. 
 
g. A fire control and prevention plan shall be provided as outlined in the Uniform Fire Code 
and as required by the local Fire District or State Fire Marshall. 
 
h. Documentation confirming an Interconnection Agreement (or equivalent 
documentation). 
 
i. Decommissioning. 
 
i. Decommissioning Plan.  Per Navajo County Code, Applicant shall be required 
to and shall address the removal of the facilities and the restoration o f the site.  
Removal of the facilities and restoration of the site shall mean that all safety 
hazards created by the installation and operation of the Solar Facility shall be 
removed and the site shall be restored to its pre -project condition to the extent  
reasonably possible, including the removal of foundations and footings to 36” 
below grade and the revegetation of any roads created or other areas graded or 
disturbed during the project.  The applicant shall provide a decommissioning plan 
prior to the commencement of construction. 
 
ii. Decommissioning Bond.  The Applicant shall maintain a decommissioning bond 
in the amount of the full decommissioning cost at the end of the anticipated life 
of the project, net of salvage value, as estimated by a Professional En gineer 
registered in the State of Arizona.  Said bond shall be reviewed and approved as 
to form, substance, and amount by the Public Works Department.  The 
Engineer’s estimate of decommissioning cost shall be renewed no less than every 
five years by a Prof essional Engineer registered in the State of Arizona, and a 
copy of each renewed estimate shall be provided to the Public Works Department

<!-- page 214 -->
for review and approval.  The decommissioning bond shall be adjusted in 
accordance with the renewed cost estimate wit hin 30 days after approval by the 
Public Works Department.  The Applicant shall provide proof that the bond is in 
place on or by the tenth (10th) anniversary of the Operations Date.  The bond shall 
be provided for the benefit of Navajo County and all priva te lessors on whose 
land any portion of the project will be located.  
 
iii. Applicant Decommissioning.  Within 12 months after the cessation of use of the 
Solar Facility for electrical power generation or transmission, the applicant, or its 
successor, at its sole cost and expense, shall decommission the Solar Facility in 
accordance with the decommissioning plan approved by the County. 
  
4. Floodplain Use Permits (where required) shall be obtained through the Navajo County Flood 
Control District prior to any construction. 
 
5. Development and use of the site shall include efforts, consistent with Best Management 
Practices, to reduce and mitigate dust created by this project during construction and operation.  
Efforts shall also be made to reduce the removal of veg etation during construction and use of 
the site and any roadways that provide access to the site. 
 
6. All outdoor lighting shall be fully shielded and shall be directed downward and shall be activated 
by motion detectors except where used for ongoing operatio ns or maintenance, in which case 
such lighting should be shielded, cantered, or cut to ensure that light reaches only areas needing 
illumination. 
 
7. Project fencing, if applicable, shall include minimum 18 -inch by 18-inch signs warning of the 
presence of high voltage.  Such signs shall be located a maximum of 300 feet apart and at all 
points of site ingress and egress.  If there is no fencing surrounding portions of the project, such 
warning signs shall be placed on each transformer building and all points of ingress and egress. 
 
8. The maximum height of project fencing shall be a minimum of six feet and a maximum of eight 
feet in height (excluding barbed wire or cyclone wire fencing, which are permissible). 
 
9. Material changes or modifications to this development or the site plan shall require an 
amendment to this Special Use Permit by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors through the 
public hearing process.  Non-material changes that do not alter the approved scope of the project 
may be administratively approved by the Navajo County Public Works Department. 
 
10. Annual Development Report.  The Applicant shall provide annual development reports to the 
County prior to construction. 
 
11. Inspections.  The applicant will allow designated County representatives or employees 
reasonable access to the facility for inspection purposes as set forth in their application. 
 
12. Compliance.  The Solar Facility shall be designed, constructed, and tested to meet relevant local, 
state, and federal standards as applicable. 
 
13. Access to the project site, via McClaws Road, by construction equipment and heavy loads could 
damage this residential roadway.  The County will record the condition of the roadways prior to 
commencement of construction operations of both the limited and full construction acti vities.  
The applicant shall participate in repairs of damage that exceeds normal wear and tear of these 
roadways prior to Certificate of Occupancy.

<!-- page 215 -->
14. Development and operation of this site shall be in full compliance with the conditions as noted 
above for this Special Use Permit.  Failure to develop and/or operate the site in full compliance 
may result in appropriate enforcement action, including, without limitation, injunctive relief.

RE: Site Plan Amendment to Resolution Number 28-19 Approving Special Use Permit for  
Hashknife Energy Center LLC
Pursuant to the Navajo County Board of Supervisors’ Resolution Number 28-19 (“the 
Resolution”) approving a Special Use Permit for Hashknife Energy Center LLC (“Hashknife 
Solar”), the applicant requests a Site Plan Amendment to incorporate an additional 840 acres 
immediately adjacent to the original project boundary. The parcels are owned by Aztec Land 
Company, LLC and are described as the South Vz of 36 and all of Section 5 in Township 18, Range 
19 East of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Joseph City Area (APN 107-05-31C and 110- 
15-008, respectively). No other modifications to the original scope of the approved resolution are 
necessary.
The Resolution approved a Special Use Permit for Hashknife Energy Center LLC to 
construct and operate an approximately 3,000 acre, 400 MW solar energy generation facility. 
Hashknife Solar requests a Site Plan Amendment to add 840 acres to the original site plan acres. 
The new parcels feature the same zoning and Comprehensive Plan Character as those approved in 
the Resolution. Please see Exhibit A for the Site Plan Amendment that incorporates the additional 
acreage.
Hashknife Solar’s Special Use Permit is subject to the conditions outlined in the 
Resolution. Among these conditions were requirements that the applicant “Limit grading to the 
greatest extent practicable...[and] take advantage of natural flow patterns.” (Navajo County Board 
of Supervisors’ Resolution Number 28-19 Approval Condition 3(e)(iii-iv)). The requested site plan 
amendment will allow greater engineering and design flexibility to minimize impacts to the site.
If you have any questions regarding the above, please contact Quentin Stuart via phone at 
812.325.2757 or by e-mail at qstuart@invenergyllc.com.

NAVAJO N  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: November 12, 2019 
Case No:  19-82 
Action:   Special Use Permit  
Applicant/Owner: Invenergy Solar Development North America LLC 
Project Name: Hashknife Solar Special Use Permit 
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 Address: No Address 
 
 APN:  107-01-023, 107-05-031C & D, & 110-15-001D 
 
 Legal Desc: Township 18 North, Range 18 East, Section 36, Township 18 North, Range 19 
  East, Sections 30, 31, & 32, & Township 17 North, Range 19 East, Section 5 of 
  the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Joseph City area.  
 
 District: III 
 
 Area:  Joseph City, AZ 
 
 Parcel Size: Approximately 3,000 acres in total. 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  
Invenergy Solar Development North America LLC has requested a Special Use Permit to allow them 
to construct a 400 MW solar energy generation and storage facility. 
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:  
 
Land in the vicinity of the project is either zoned RU-20 or is State land, which is considered under the 
RU-1 zoning category in Navajo County for planning purposes.  For the most part, the surrounding land 
is vacant land being used for grazing.  The nearest residence is approximately one and one -half (1 ½) 
miles away.  Cholla Power Plant, at is closest, is just under two (2) miles away from the proposed 
facility.

<!-- page 228 -->
HISTORY: 
 
The subject properties for this project have historically been used as grazing land.  The current owners 
of these properties are  the State of Arizona and Aztec Land and Cattle Company.  The properties are 
currently vacant and have never had any permits related to Planning, Zoning, and Building.   
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
The applicant is proposing a photovoltaic solar and storage energy facility south of I -40 and Joseph 
City.  The proposed project is set to span approximately 3,000 acres and produce approximately 400 
MW of electricity.   The applicant projects that approximately 200 -400 jobs will be supported during 
construction and 3-5 full-time positions will be supported during operations of the facility. 
 
Projections by the applicant have the project beginning limited construction activities in November

<!-- page 229 -->
2019.  Plans for limited construction activities are to install approximately eleven (11) 10’ x 40’ inverter 
pile foundations.  Each inverter foundation will consist of 27 steel piles arranged in a 10’ x 40’ rectangle.  
Total ground coverage of the inverter foundations is expected to be approximately 4,500 square feet 
(0.10 acres).  No concrete or civil work will be required for limited construction activities.  
 
Full Construction is not planned to commence until early 2022, with, with completion and full 
commercial operation scheduled in  the last quarter of 2022.  During this construction, there will be a 
laydown yard for staging and storage that will be used to house temporary office trailers.  Portable 
toilets will be provided for use with this area.  The applicant has requested a five-year (5) construction 
period from the approval of the Special Use Permit to allow for full completion of construction. 
 
Operations facilities are proposed for this project, including an operations and ma intenance building, 
control building, energy storage buildings, a storage yard, and parking areas.  The operations and 
maintenance building is proposed to be 1,200 square feet and will not exceed thirty (30) feet.   The site 
for the operations and ma intenance facilities has also been indicated as the working location for the 
temporary construction laydown yard. 
 
Photovoltaic energy will be collected using ground -mounted photovoltaic solar panels.   The panels 
being proposed are 10-foot tall rack mounted single-axis tracking photovoltaic solar panels.   
 
Beyond those already mentioned, other indicated project components are interior access roads and a 
perimeter road, inverters and a collection system (not to exceed 15 feet in height), a collector substation, 
security fencing, a distribution line for constr uction and operation power, and a 500 -kV generation-tie 
line or bus connection. 
 
The project is slated to connect to the electrical grid via a 3.5 -mile generation tie line to the point of 
interconnection at the Cholla substation. 
 
A six-foot chain-link fence topped with one-foot security barbed wire will be installed around facilities 
as they are constructed and access to the site will be controlled by gates.  Signage and separate fencing 
will be used for high-voltage equipment. 
 
Lighting on this project is designed to be downward facing, with motion -activated security lighting to 
monitor the collector substation, control enclosure, and solar arrays.  There may be the need for 
temporary lighting facilities during construction. 
 
Water for this project will be  necessary during the construction phases of this project for the purposes 
of compaction and dust control.  Additionally, there will need to be water for reclamation and concrete 
mixing for foundations.  The applicant intends to obtain water offsite or through a lease agreement with 
the owners of applicable water sources. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE 4 – (RURAL 20) – RURAL ZONING DISTRICT 
 TWENTY (20) ACRES PER DWELLING UNIT 
 
Section 401 – Purpose 
 
The primary purpose of this district is to conserve and protect open land uses, foster orderly growth in 
rural areas, and prevent urban agricultural land use conflicts.  The purpose of requiring large minimum

<!-- page 230 -->
parcels of not less than twenty (20) acres in a rea is to discourage small lot or residential subdivisions 
where public facilities such as water, sewage disposal, parks and playgrounds, and governmental 
services such as police and fire protection are not available or could not reasonably be made current ly 
available.  Uses permitted in this zoning district include both farm and non-farm residential uses, farms, 
and recreational and institutional uses. 
 
ARTICLE 8 – (RURAL 1) RURAL ZONING DISTRICT 
 ONE (1) ACRE PER DWELLING UNIT 
 
Section 801 – Purpose  
 
The primary purpose of this district is to conserve and protect open land uses, foster orderly growth in 
rural areas, and prevent urban agricultural land use conflicts.  The purpose of requiring large minimum 
parcels of not less than one (1) acre in area is to discourage small lot or residential subdivisions where 
public facilities such as water, sewage disposal, parks and playgrounds, and governmental services such 
as police and fire protection are not available or could not reasonably be made currently available.  Uses 
permitted in this zoning district include both farm and non-farm residential uses, farms, and recreational 
and institutional uses. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
  
The Board of Supervisors may permit as a Special Use the following uses in zoning districts 
from which they are otherwise prohibited by this Ordinance, unless any such use is further 
prohibited by this Article. 
 
 13. Electric power generating plants and facili ties, including but not limited to those 
 operated by nuclear or fossil fuel or solar, wind or geothermal energy. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
Comprehensive Plan 
 
The majority of  the property for the proposed project falls into the Rural Edge character area.  The 
purpose of the Rural Edge character area is to provide lower density residential development adjacent 
to community development.  If any industrial or storage uses are est ablished in the Rural Edge area, 
they should be screened from views from the rights-of-way. 
 
Land Use Element 
 
The Land Use Element for Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan mentions that planning for land use 
in the County needs to include consideration for air quality and access to incident solar and wind energy 
for all character areas. 
 
Goal 3:  To preserve and protect archaeological and historic resources for their aesthetic, 
scientific, educational, and cultural value.

<!-- page 231 -->
Policy 3-A: Where probable cause for discovery of cultural or archaeological resources exists, 
 encourage property owner(s) to contact the Arizona State Historic Preservation Office. 
 
- The applicant has already completed a desktop review of cultural resourc es to determine 
previously recorded cultural resources.  This review included a cultural resource file search 
from AZSITE, NPS-National Register of Historic Places (NHRP) Database, Arizona State 
Register of Historic Places (SRHP), General Land Office (GLO)  Plat Maps, Original Land 
Patent Data, Cemetery location data, The U.S. Department of Agriculture Natural Resources 
Conservation Service (NRCS) Soil, and the Survey Geographic Database.  The majority of 
the site has never been surveyed for the presence of cultural resource materials.  The result 
of the research completed by the applicant will be incorporated into the development and 
design of their project.  If cultural resources are encountered, there will be a temporary 
cessation of the project at that lo cation until an archaeologist can assess the significance of 
the findings. 
 
Goal 5: Improve the overall appearance of the County. 
 
 Policy 5 -A: Maintain natural scenic qualities of the County by identifying and protecting 
 cultural resources; protecting wildlife habitat; natural plant communities and riparian areas; 
 and encouraging scenic vistas. 
 
- Pre-construction surveys have been co mpleted to assess wildlife and habitat resources.  
Surveys for the vegetation have shown there is a low likelihood of a federally listed species 
within the project area.  Additionally, the AGFD Habitat Evaluation and Lands Program 
Specialist for Region 1 h as stated that the habitat within the project is not well -suited for 
pronghorn.  Pronghorn densities within the project area are significantly lower than those to 
the south of the area.  There are several ephemeral washes found, which will be accounted 
for with layout design in order to be avoided to the greatest extent possible.  As far as scenic 
qualities, the project is located to the south of Cholla Power Plant, which already has a large 
impact on the nearby view corridor.  The project will be visible f rom I-40, however, given 
the vegetation and alternatives for screening, there is little to no possibility of screening the 
project without making the project equally as impactful on the view corridor. 
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
 
 Policy 6-B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses including, 
 but not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping. 
 
- Buffers for this project do not seem viable, given the shrubby vegetation and scope of the 
project.  Attempts at screening would make the project as visible to the surroundings as they 
would if no screening were employed. 
 
 Policy 6-C: Require paved or other appropriately surfaced access to commercial and indu strial 
 uses. 
 
- Up until about 2 miles prior to where the project will be accessed, there is paved access 
through Obed Road and McLaws Road.  For the remaining distance of road, which is 
dirt/gravel, the applicant has indicated that there will be a dust -mitigation plan to ensure 
there are not dust issues with the usage of the roadways during construction.  Following the

<!-- page 232 -->
completion of construction, there should be relatively little impact on any of the roadways 
used to access the project site. 
 
Goal 7: Facilitate commercial and industrial development, including business and technology 
uses, where appropriate to increase the sales tax and employment opportunities. 
 
 Policy 7-B: Encourage the development of non -resource specific industrial uses along major 
 roadways, rail heads, and airports and the development of resource specific industrial uses in 
 appropriate remote locations. 
 
- This project could be considered reso urce specific in nature.  Given that it is located to the 
south of Joseph City and Cholla Power Plant, it is in a location that is fairly remote, yet has 
the infrastructure and potential to make for a suitable site for large-scale operations. 
 
Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner. 
 
 Policy 9 -D: Encourage in -fill opportunities in the County to capitalize on existing 
 infrastructure and reduce some growth-related costs. 
 
- This project aims to utilize the already existing electrical infrastructure that has been 
installed in the area for Cholla Power Plant.  The current intent of the plan is to connect the 
project collector substation to the Cholla substation. 
 
Circulation Element 
 
Goal 1: Provide a range of  circulation options that are safe and efficient and that complement 
local communities and the natural environment. 
 
 Policy 1 -B: Coordinate land use and circulation planning to encourage comprehensive and 
 efficient development and growth patterns that su pport adjacent land uses, complement the
 character of adjacent communities and neighborhoods, and mitigate impacts on the natural
 environment. 
 
Area Plan 
 
The proposed area for the project is within the boundaries of the Aztec Area Plan for Navajo County, 
and as such, this plan must be consulted when reviewing projects of this nature within the area.  Within 
the Aztec Area Plan, the properties for the project are located within the Aztec Rural Ranch character 
area and Aztec Rural Edge character area .  Both of these character areas note that utilities and energy 
generation facilities are encouraged within their designated areas.  It is noted that industrial or storage 
uses should be screened from highways and major roadway rights-of-way. 
 
The subject properties are on the edges of Planning Area M: Territorial Ranch and Planning Area N: 
Well Field District.  Planning Area M notes that due to its proximity to existing transmission lines, the 
land it contains may be suitable for major energy production facilities, such as solar or other renewable 
energy sources.  Planning Area N mentions that due to its location adjacent to numerous major power 
transmission lines, the presence of a major natural gas transmission line, and the visual dominance of 
the already existing Cholla Power Plant, this district is an excellent location for future renewable energy 
production facilities, such as solar power generation.

<!-- page 233 -->
Analysis: 
 
This project meets, or has the goals to meet, a number of the goals of the Land Use Element of the 
Navajo County Comprehensive Plan.  By working to identify any cultural resources ahead of time and 
having a plan for what to do if cultural resources are encountered, this project will be using best practices 
to preserve any cultural or historical resources on the project site.   
 
The applicant has identified flora and fauna as well as riparian areas within the project and reached out 
to the appropriate agencies to make sure that there is minimal impact to protected species.  It appears, 
based on these correspondences, that this project should have little to no impact on wildlife or any 
protected species within the general vicinity of this project. 
 
Appropriate access is located coming into this project, with the paved McLaws Road and Obed Road 
both being adjacent to the proposed site.  Public works is requesting the review of all traffic plans for 
delivery, construction, and other related activities surrounding  this project to make sure that proper 
access is achieved for the various stages of completion. 
 
The Comprehensive Plan notes that resource specific industries should be contained within 
appropriately remote areas.  This project is going to be located in a  fairly rural area, south of Joseph 
City, that still manages to take advantage of amenities like paved roads for the transportation of 
equipment. 
 
Navajo County encourages instances of infill that capitalize on existing infrastructure.  Although this 
project will not be participating in infill in the traditional sense, as a business or group of homes might, 
it will be utilizing its own form of this idea.  This project aims to use already -existing infrastructure 
surrounding Cholla Power Plant to connect to t he electrical grid.  With the imminent shut -down of 
Cholla Power Plant looming, this proposed project will look to fill some of the void presented by the 
closing of the generation station. 
 
As far as the Aztec Area Plan, under which this project falls, the re is support for the project as well.   
Both character areas under which the project site sits note that utilities and energy generation facilities 
should be encouraged.  The Planning Areas encompassing the project also make mention of the 
appropriateness to locate renewable energy production facilities within their boundaries. 
 
Invenergy hosted a public open house on Wednesday, May 15th, 2019 from 6:00 p.m. until 8:00 p.m. at 
the Joseph City Fire District Building.   Notice of this event was posted in The Holbrook Tribune on 
May 8th and May 15th.  This meeting was attended by twelve (12) individuals, including employees of 
Navajo County and Arizona Public Service.  Excluding these agencies, the sign -in sheet indicates that 
there were seven (7) members of the general public in attendance.   Following the open house, Invenergy 
had a question and comment period allowing for submittals until May 31st.  
 
As of September 18th, 2019, there have been some concerns from the public raised over the scale of the 
project, its potential impact on local grasses used for cattle grazing, and mitigation plans for the spread 
of weeds. 
 
At the Planning and Zoning Commission public hearing on the 17 th of October, this item was 
recommended for approval to the Navajo County Board of Supervisors  by a vote of 3-1 subject to the 
conditions contained within and the finalization of conditions 3d, 3i, and 10, which has been completed 
with staff, the applicant, and their respective legal advisors.

<!-- page 234 -->
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Article 29.  
 
This item was posted in the October 2 nd, 2019 edition of The Holbrook Tribune.  Additionally, 
postcards were mailed to all neighbors within 300 feet of the property  on the same date.  This 
property was posted on September 10th, 2019.  
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed project meets a wide variety of goals within Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan 
and the Aztec Area Plan, as noted within this report. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in  which the 
property is located. 
 
The proposed use is not allowed by -right in the zoning district in which the property is located, 
which is why a Special Use Permit is being requested. 
 
4. The proposed use is  compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use.  
 
The proposed use is compatible with current and likely future uses of the properties within its 
vicinity.  The property is surrounded by mostly vacant grazing land and is not far from Cholla Power 
Plant and other land that is desirable for usage for solar power generation and storage. 
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
Given the rural nature of the property and the current makeup of the nearby landscape, there should 
be minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use.

PLANNING AND ZONING COMMISSION RECOMMENDATION:

<!-- page 235 -->
The Planning and Zoning Commission has recommended the approval of this Special Use Permit to the 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. All improvements and infrastructure, including, but not limited to, solar arrays, inverters, signs, 
temporary construction offices, storage facilities, etc., shall adhere to all applicable Navajo 
County Codes, Standards, and Ordinances and are subject to applicable building permit and/or 
other permitting processes.  No building or structure may be constructed or occupied prior to 
complete compliance with all appropriate Public Works Department requirements.  This shall 
include, but not be limited to, grading and drainage plans, flood control requirements as deemed 
necessary, and the issuance of building, construction and/or other permits for all proposed 
improvements and structures. 
 
2. In the applicant’s building permit application for the limited construction activities to occur 
within 2019, the applicant shall provide a statement addressing plans for transportation and 
access, construction management (including traffic control, site access, and lighting) and 
construction mitigation.  In addition, the applicant shall submit a best management practices 
plan to Navajo County for approval, to mitigate ground disturbed and limit impact to existing 
vegetation during the limited construction activities within 2019 only. 
 
3. Prior to the issuance of any building or related permits and the commencement of construction  
occurring after 2019, the following are to be submitted for review and approval by the Navajo 
County Public Works Department. 
 
a. A Transportation and Access Plan/S tatement. The applicant shall submit a  
Transportation and Access Plan/Statement for the development of the Hashknife Solar 
Project to the Navajo County Public Works Department for review and approval.  The 
Transportation Plan shall, at a minimum, address the following: 
 
i. Site access.  Provide specific and detailed information regarding off and on -site 
roadways, including roadway widths and surfacing.  Also, if deemed necessary, 
provide additional information regarding existing on -site roadways/easements, 
including title reports, other documentation regarding the roadway(s), exhibits, 
and/or legal descriptions indicating the existing and proposed alignments.  Staff 
notes, and the applicant acknowledges, that the realignment, abandonment and/or 
dedication of roadways may require separate action by the Navajo County Board 
of Supervisors. 
 
b. Construction Management Plan.  The Applicant shall prepare a Construction 
Management Plan for the Solar Facility.  The plan shall address the following: 
 
i. Traffic control methods (in coordination with the Department of Transportation 
prior to initiation of construction), including lane closures, signage, and flagging 
procedures. 
 
ii. Site access planning directing employee and delivery traffic to minimize conflicts 
with local traffic. 
 
iii. Lighting. During construction of the Solar Facility, any temporary construction 
lighting shall be positioned downward, inward, and shielded to eliminate glare

<!-- page 236 -->
from all adjacent properties.  Emergency and safety lighting shall be exempt from 
this condition. 
 
c. Construction Mitigation Plan. The applicant shall prepare a Construction Mitigation Plan 
for the Solar Facility.  This plan shall address, at a minimum, the effective mitigation of 
dust, hours of construction activity, access and road improveme nts, and handling of 
general construction complaints. 
 
d. Revegetation/Restoration Plan.  A revegetation and restoration plan shall be prepared 
per Arizona Department of Environmental Quality (ADEQ) Storm Water Pollution 
Prevention Plan and Stormwater Permit requirements.  The applicant will consult with 
the landowners and National Resource Conservation Service (NRCS) as to the 
composition of the seed mix.  The revegetation and restoration plan must address any 
areas which may be disturbed during the construct ion and/or operation of the facility.  
This plan must also address the areas underneath the solar arrays. 
 
e. Grading and Drainage Plan.  The Solar Facility shall be constructed in compliance with 
the County -approved grading plan as determined and approved by  the Planning and 
Zoning Director or their designee prior to the commencement of any construction 
activities.  The grading plan shall: 
 
i. Clearly show existing and proposed contours 
 
ii. Note the locations and amount of soil to be removed, if any, and the percen t of 
the site to be graded 
 
iii. Limit grading to the greatest extent practicable by avoiding steep slopes and 
laying out arrays parallel to landforms 
 
iv. Take advantage of natural flow patterns in drainage design and keep the amount 
of impervious surface as low as possible to reduce stormwater storage needs. 
 
f. Wildlife Corridors.  The applicant shall identify an access corridor for wildlife to 
navigate through the Solar Facility.  The proposed wildlife corridor shall be shown on 
the site plan submitted to the County.  Areas between fencing shall be kept open to allow 
for the movement of migratory animals and other wildlife.   Width of corridor to be 
determined. 
 
g. A fire control and prevention plan shall be provided as outlined in the Uniform Fire Code 
and as required by the local Fire District or State Fire Marshall. 
 
h. Documentation confirming an Interconnection Agreement (or equivalent 
documentation). 
 
i. Decommissioning. 
 
i. Decommissioning Plan.  Per Navajo County Code, Applicant shall be required 
to and shall address the removal of the facilities and the restoration of the site.  
Removal of the facilities and restoration of the site shall mean that all safety 
hazards created by the installation and operation of the Solar Facility shall be

<!-- page 237 -->
removed and the site shall be restored to its pre -project condition to the extent 
reasonably possible, including the removal of foundations and footings to 36” 
below grade and the revegetati on of any roads created or other areas graded or 
disturbed during the project.  The applicant shall provide a decommissioning plan 
prior to the commencement of construction. 
 
ii. Decommissioning Bond.  The Applicant shall maintain a decommissioning bond 
in the amount of the full decommissioning cost at the end of the anticipated life 
of the project, net of salvage value, as estimated by a Professional Engineer 
registered in the State of Arizona.  Said bond shall be reviewed and approved as 
to form, substance, a nd amount by the Public Works Department.  The 
Engineer’s estimate of decommissioning cost shall be renewed no less than every 
five years by a Professional Engineer registered in the State of Arizona, and a 
copy of each renewed estimate shall be provided to the Public Works Department 
for review and approval.  The decommissioning bond shall be adjusted in 
accordance with the renewed cost estimate within 30 days after approval by the 
Public Works Department.  The Applicant shall provide proof that the bond is in 
place on or by the tenth (10th) anniversary of the Operations Date.  The bond shall 
be provided for the benefit of Navajo County and all private lessors on whose 
land any portion of the project will be located.  
 
iii. Applicant Decommissioning.  Within 12 months after the cessation of use of the 
Solar Facility for electrical power generation or transmission, the applicant, or its 
successor, at its sole cost and expense, shall decommission the Solar Facility in 
accordance with the decommissioning plan approved by the County. 
  
4. Floodplain Use Permits (where required) shall be obtained through the Navajo County Flood 
Control District prior to any construction. 
 
5. Development and use of the site shall include efforts, consistent with Be st Management 
Practices, to reduce and mitigate dust created by this project during construction and operation.  
Efforts shall also be made to reduce the removal of vegetation during construction and use of 
the site and any roadways that provide access to the site. 
 
6. All outdoor lighting shall be fully shielded and shall be directed downward and shall be activated 
by motion detectors except where used for ongoing operations or maintenance, in which case 
such lighting should be shielded, cantered, or cut to ensure that light reaches only areas needing 
illumination. 
 
7. Project fencing, if applicable, shall include minimum 18 -inch by 18-inch signs warning of the 
presence of high voltage.  Such signs shall be located a maximum of 300 feet apart and at all 
points of site ingress and egress.  If there is no fencing surrounding portions of the project, such 
warning signs shall be placed on each transformer building and all points of ingress and egress. 
 
8. The maximum height of project fencing shall be a minimum of six feet and a maximum of eight 
feet in height (excluding barbed wire or cyclone wire fencing, which are permissible). 
 
9. Material changes or modifications to this development or the site plan shall require an 
amendment to this Special Use Permit by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors through the 
public hearing process.  Non-material changes that do not alter the approved scope of the project

<!-- page 238 -->
may be administratively approved by the Navajo County Public Works Department. 
 
10. Annual Development Report.  The Applicant shall provide annual development reports to the 
County prior to construction. 
 
11. Inspections.  The applicant will allow designated County representatives or employees  
reasonable access to the facility for inspection purposes as set forth in their application. 
 
12. Compliance.  The Solar Facility shall be designed, constructed, and tested to meet relevant local, 
state, and federal standards as applicable. 
 
13. Access to the project site, via McClaws Road, by construction equipment and heavy loads could 
damage this residential roadway.  The County will record the condition of the roadways prior to 
commencement of construction ope rations of both the limited and full construction activities.  
The applicant shall participate in repairs of damage that exceeds normal wear and tear of these 
roadways prior to Certificate of Occupancy. 
 
14. Development and operation of this site shall be in f ull compliance with the conditions as noted 
above for this Special Use Permit.  Failure to develop and/or operate the site in full compliance 
may result in appropriate enforcement action, including, without limitation, injunctive relief.

12. Compliance.  The Solar Facility shall be designed, constructed, and tested to meet 
relevant local, state, and federal standards as applicable. 
 
13. Access to the project site, via McC laws Road, by construction equipment and 
heavy loads could damage this resident ial roadway.  The County will record the 
condition of the roadways prior to commencement of construction operations of 
both the limited and full construction activities.  The ap plicant shall participate in 
repairs of damage that exceeds normal wear and tea r of these roadways prior to 
Certificate of Occupancy. 
 
14. Development and operation of this site shall be in full compliance with the 
conditions as noted above for this Special Us e Permit.  Failure to develop and/or 
operate the site in full compliance may re sult in appropriate enforcement action, 
including, without limitation, injunctive relief.

A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING 
A SPECIAL USE PERMIT AMENDMENT FOR HASHKNIFE ENERGY CENTER LLC  
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit Amendment was duly filed on 
February 24, 2020 by Hashkn ife Energy Center LLC to expand the site area for the construction 
and operation of a 400 MW solar energy generation and storage facility from the previously 
approved 3,000 acres to approximately 3,960 acres; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the foll owing real p roperty in Navajo County: 
APN: 107 -01-023, 107 -05-031C, 107 -05-031D, 110 -15-001D, and 110 -15-008, which ae 
portions of Township 18 North, Range 19 East, Section 30, 31, and 32, and Township 17 North, 
Range 19 East, Sections 5 and 6 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Joseph City area; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on June 18, 2020; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after c onsidering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of staff, found that 
the Special Use Permit  Amendment is not consistent with the public health, safety and general 
welfare and should be denied; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors  hereby 
deny the Special Use Permit Amendment.

Board of Supervisors Regular 10. f. 2.        
Meeting Date: 06/23/2020  
Title: AJ's Getaway RV Park-Request Approval of Special Use Permit-District IV
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Berrin Nejad
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 21-2020 
approving/denying a Special Use Permit for AJ's Getaway RV Park, for construction and
operation of a recreational vehicle (RV) park, consisting of 43 RV-camp sites and 30 spaces for
RV and boat storage on 14.83 acres of land, located at the northeast corner of Highway 277 and
Buckskin Road.
Background:
The property is zoned C-R (Commercial-Residential), addressed as 3537 Buckskin, in the Heber
area.    APN#: 206-34-003A, T12N, R17E, S20, District IV.
The applicant is requesting a Special Use Permit approval to construct and operate an RV Park
with 43 RV camp sites and a storage facility for 30 RV and/or boats.  The property is located on
the northeast corner of Highway 277 and Buckskin Road, 14.83 acres in size and zoned as
Commercial-Residential (C-R), surrounded by residential on north, east, and Buckskin Road,
commercial along Hwy 277.  The park will have access from Buckskin Road and an emergency
ingress/egress will be located on the east side, accessing Highway 277.  The existing structure
will remain as applicants' residence.
At the entrance, there will be a 200 square-foot check-in area with 15 parking spaces. 
Additionally, a large ramada with picnic tables, three corral style camping circles on the northern
portion of the site, two with 3-4 RV's and one with 6-8 RVs will be.  The entire park will have
7-foot cedar fencing around the property.    The quiet hours will be from 10pm to 7am daily.   The
business is seasonal and there will be a sign when closed for the winter.
The first neighborhood meeting was held on February 3, 2020, and the second one was on April 2,
2020, via Facebook.   Due to technical difficulties with the internet connection for this meeting,
the neighbors prepared and sent questions to the applicant after the meeting. These questions and
applicants' answers are attached. (Attachment 8)
Planning and Zoning Commission held their regular meeting on May 21, 2020.  Several
neighbors connected to the meeting and raised some concerns regarding additional traffic, ATV's
speeding, dust, and potential of reduced property values.

<!-- page 254 -->
After hearing the presentation and considering all comments, Commission voted 5-0 to
recommend approval to the Board of Supervisors.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of the Special Use Permit for AJ's Getaway RV Park.

NAVAJO COUNTY 
 
               COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT DEPARTMENT 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
 
 
TO:    Planning and Zoning Commission 
 
FROM:   Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director 
  
THROUGH:    John Osgood, Public Works Director 
 
HEARING DATE:  May 21, 2020  
 
PROJECT NUMBER: SUP 20-001    AJ’s Getaway RV Park 
 
The applicant is requesting an approval for a Special Use Permit to allow construction and operation 
of a recreational vehicle (RV) park, consisting of 43 RV-camp sites and 30 spaces for RV and boat 
storage on 14.83 acres of land, located at the northeast corner of Highway 277 a nd Buckskin Road .  
The property is zoned C-R (Commercial-Residential), addressed as 3537 Buckskin, in the Heber area.    
APN#: 206-34-003A, T12N, R17E, S20, District IV.  
 
PROJECT DATA and FACTS: 
Request: Special Use Permit approval 
Applicant: Aaron and Wilma James 
Agent: Painted Sky Engineering & Surveying, LLC  
Zoning and Land Use:  C-R:  Commercial-Residential Zoning District.  The 
existing structure will be utilized by the property/project 
owner as their residence.  
 
District: IV 
 
 
LAND USE: 
North: Residential, A -General 
East: Residential, C -R 
South: Highway 277  
West: Buckskin Road and then residential, R1-10

<!-- page 256 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission – May 21, 2020 
2 | P a g e  
 
 
PROCEDURAL HISTORY: 
 
1- A neighborhood meeting was held on February 3, 2020 by the applicant.  
 
2- On March 2, 2020, postcards were mailed to all neighbors within 300 feet of the project.  
 
3- The public hearing notice for this application was published in The Holbrook Tribune Newspaper 
on Wednesday, March 4, 2020. 
 
4- The physical site was posted on March 3, 2020 with the public hearing date, time and location. 
 
5- The March 19, 2020 meeting was cancelled due to COVID-19 virus. 
 
6- Second Neighborhood meeting was held by the applicant via Facebook on April 2, 2020.  
 
7- For May 21, 2020 meeting via Zoom; notifications sent out to the neighbors on May 4, 2020. 
 
8- The site was posted on May 6, 2020 for the May 21, 2020 meeting. 
 
 
PROJECT PROPOSAL:  
 
Background: 
The proposed development is for a 43-unit short-term RV park on 14.83 acres of land, zoned a s C-R 
(Commercial-Residential) along State Route 277  for 300 feet and the remaining land is zoned A-
General.  The property is located at the northeast corner of Highway 277 and Buckskin Road in Heber. 
 
The surrounding properties are commercial on SR 277 and residential along Buckskin Road, adjacent 
to the north and the east.    
 
The main entrance to the site w ill be from Buckskin Road  and an emergency ingress/egress will be 
located on the east side of th e development, access ing Highway 277.  The internal development site 
circulation system is designed in such a way that the RVs would come into the site and drive through 
the park or leave without making any U-turns.  Each individual camping site will have a 50 amp plug 
capability with water and sewer hookups.   
 
There is an existing structure on the property which will remain as the owners’ residence.  The check-
in area will have 15 parking spaces with one being  handicap accessible in the entrance of the park.   
The clubhouse and the check-in building with a wheelchair accessible restroom is around 200 square 
feet in size with a small convenient store for the guests that will provide items like coffee, hotdogs, 
toothpaste, ice cream, etc.  The pavilion will be ramada style, 40 ft x 40 ft x 15 ft, open on the sides 
with picnic tables. The quiet hours will be  from 10 pm to 7 am daily.   The workshop will be two 
standard shipping containers, 8 .5 ft x 40 ft, which will also be used for storage purposes.  
 
There will be a corral style camping circle on the northern portion of the development. Two of them 
are for smaller groups (3-4 RVs) and one is for larger group camping (6-8 RVs).

<!-- page 257 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission – May 21, 2020 
3 | P a g e  
 
The development also will have 30 storage units along Hwy 277 for boats and RVs.  The sizes of the 
storage spaces will be 48 ft deep and 12 ft wide.  
 
Business will be open seasonally.  During the time it is closed, there will be a CLOSED sign on the 
front, visible from Hwy 277.  In case of a mistaken turn onto Buckskin Road in the off season, the site 
plan is modified to allow RVs to be able to turn around without going further in to the neighborhood. 
The modification is to set back the gate 35 feet from the fence so that it will provide a little over 52 
feet from the existing east edge of Buckskin Road to the gate , which will allow a 45-foot vehicle to 
turn around comfortably. 
 
Buffering:  Solid 7-foot wood cedar fencing will be installed around the entire perimeter of the 
property.  
 
Parking: All parking will be within the project boundaries.  
 
Trash:   The trash container will be located inside the property boundary, easily a ccessible from 
Buckskin Road for the regular trash service to the site. 
 
Landscaping:  The site naturally has several existing juniper trees, shrubs and other trees.  These will 
be preserved as much as possible for preservation and for the enjoyment of the visitors.  Some 
additional vegetation will be  planted when/if necessary. As it is, current vegetation meets the code 
requirements.   
 
Signage:  The development will have signage at the corner of Hwy 277 and Buckskin Road identifying 
the location.  The same sign will be used for the off season when the park is closed to inform drivers.  
The sign will meet the County sign code.  
 
Letters for Support or Against to the Project:  Staff received fifteen letters in support of the project 
and one (1) in opposition, no reasons explained.  
 
 
APPLICABLE LAW, REPORTS and DOCUMENTS: 
 
o NAVAJO COUNTY, ZONING ORDINANCE: 
The following reports and Sections are applicable to the proposed project.  
 
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT 
Section 1501 – Purpose. 
 
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services in convenient 
locations to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential neighborhoods and 
wholesaling or distribution activities in locations where there is adequate access to major stre ets or 
highways.  Principal uses permitted in this zoning district include retail and wholesale commerce, 
commercial entertainment, and some uses of a light manufacturing nature. 
 
ARTICLE 20 - SPECIAL USES and PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENTS 
Section 2001 – Special Uses

<!-- page 258 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission – May 21, 2020 
4 | P a g e  
 
28. Recreational Vehicle parks, subject to all the regulations applicable to RV parks, 
specified in the use regulations for the “R-3” zoning district. 
 
32. Storage of manufactured homes, travel trailers, boats and aircraft. 
 
o COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN: 
 
The subject site is located within the Heber -Overgaard area. Therefore, the Heber-Overgaard Area 
Plan must be consulted with this request. 
 
Heber-Overgaard Area Plan: 
Goal: Benefit from our natural resources through the development of the tourism industry. 
 
 Objective 1: Establish recreational development.  
  Implementation Strategy 2:  Encourage private enterprise to support and establish 
  recreational development in and around our area.  
 
Through the addition of Recreational Vehicle Park and a storage facility, this proposal 
would be supporting the recreational activities in the Heber-Overgaard area. 
 
 
Goal: Provide residential areas that are composed of quiet, safe neighborhoods that have easy access 
to schools, retail establishments, employment, and service establishments. 
 
 Objective 3:  Provide each recreational vehicle in RV Parks with its own pad and utility 
 hookups. 
 
 Each space will have its own pad and utility hookups.  
 
 Objective 5: Provide vegetative buffer zone along the perimeter of the manufactured homes 
 areas and RV Parks. 
 
  Implementation Strategy 3: Support continual review and updating of zoning to  
  separate general residential, manufactured home parks, RV Parks, and mult i-family 
  areas from each other with greenbelts or other buffer zones. 
 
There is a natural landscape in the vicinity and the property  as well as a buffer land 
with vegetation between the property and State Route 277.  This area will be kept as 
natural buffer up to the fencing area.   
 
 
4.0 Land Use Element 
 
4.7 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
Policy 6 -C: Require paved or other appropriately surfaced access to commercial and 
industrial uses.

<!-- page 259 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission – May 21, 2020 
5 | P a g e  
 
 
The proposed use will be commercial but still has residential component to it.  The business 
operation will be seasonal and will be operated only when the weather is suitable for outdoors 
and camping.   Other times, the site will be closed, except for special events.  The Buckskin 
Road is County maintained dirt road , reviewed by the engineering department and found 
suitable for the proposed use.   
 
Goal 7: Facilitate commercial and industrial development, including business and technology uses, 
where appropriate to increase the sales tax and employment opportunities.  
Policy 7-A: Encourage strategic placement of commercial development so as to attract and 
locate shopping and employment uses that have paved or other appropriately surfaced access 
and are easily accessible, particularly in or near established communities and cities, and in  
communities with air and/or rail access. 
 
This proposed development is located along State Route 277 and Buckskin Road intersectio n 
with an easy access to the site and located among the other commercial uses along the highway. 
Being open to business only in certain months of the year and the type of business that includes 
family camping makes the proposal a good buffer from commercial uses and highway traffic 
to low density residential.  
 
Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner. 
Policy 9-B: Encourage higher density and intensity development in those areas of the County 
where services, including, but not limited to, water and sewer systems, paved rights -of-way, 
and schools, are available or can be created with new development.  
 
This development is commercial and will bring visitors to the area during the summer season.  
The proposal will have water and sanitary system to the site, the impact will be seasonal on the 
infrastructure and road system, but there will be no impact on school system.  
 
5.0 Circulation Element 
 
5.4 Goals and Policies: 
 
Goal 4: Provide for safe travel and access to property. 
 Policy 4-B: Continue working with developers and property owners to achieve safe and legal 
 access for all properties. 
 
The property already has direct and safe access through the existing thoroughfares.  
 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised 
Statutes and Article 29.  
 
Postcards were sent out for this item on Monday, May 2, 2020 and the property was posted and a legal 
ad notice in The Holbrook Tribune was published on Wednesday, May 6, 2020.

<!-- page 260 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission – May 21, 2020 
6 | P a g e  
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
This proposal is in compliance with policies and goals of the Heber-Overgaard Area Plan and Navajo 
County Comprehensive Plan.  For those policies and goals not met yet, staff has recommended 
conditions that would allow it to meet these policies and goals. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which 
the property is located. 
 
The subject property for the proposed use is C-R Commercial-Residential, and as such, the proposed 
use is compatible with other commercial uses. 
 
4. The proposed use is  compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in 
the vicinity of the proposed use.  
 
The proposed use is located along a highway, surrounded by residential uses with a street dividing the 
west side.  The proposed RV Park is seasonal and is compatible with surrounding uses while serving 
as a buffer from the highway traffic and noise.  
 
5. The proposed use will  have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and 
general welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
The proposal was reviewed by several departments and agencies for public health, safety, and general 
welfare.  Additional stipulations listed to minimize the adverse effect to neighboring properties by the 
proposed use.   
 
 
 
OTHER COUNTY DEPARTMENT’S and AGENCY COMMENTS: 
 
• FIRE DEPARTMENT:  A note from Chief Dee McCluskey of Heber-Overgaard Fire District 
is on file with their service availability to the proposed site.     
 
• DRAINAGE & FLOOD CONTROL: 
The Flood Control staff recommendations and/or stipulations shall be met before any permit  
is issued.         
 
• ENGINEERING AND TRAFFIC:  All the engineering recommendations and/or stipulatio ns 
shall be met before any permit is issued.  
 
  
   
STIPULATIONS:  
 
A Special Use Permit is proposed to allow the development of an RV Park with a storage area for RVs 
and boats in a C-R (Commercial -Residential) Zone.  If approved, staff recommends the following 
stipulations:

<!-- page 261 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission – May 21, 2020 
7 | P a g e  
 
1. That the project is developed in conformance with the development plans submitted for the 
May 21, 2020 Navajo County, Planning and Zoning Commission meeting and may be further 
modified by the Planning and Zoning Commission.  
 
2. That the project conforms with a ll the comments and the stipulations required by other 
departments and agencies.  All stipulations shall be met before applying for any permit and/or 
issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy.   
 
3. A certificate of zoning compliance documenting the completion of all the stipulations shall be 
issued by the Planning staff prior to the Certificate of Occupancy.  
 
4. All RV park spaces shall have their own pad and utility hookups. 
 
5. All lighting on the property shall face inward, be shielded, be motion -activated, and be in 
conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance. 
 
6. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the approved site plan.  Any expansion 
or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this SUP approval. 
 
7. Seasonal shut down signs shall be clearly visible from Hwy 260  and shall be in acc ordance 
 with the Navajo County Sign  ordinance. 
 
8. Any other signage shall be according to the Navajo County  Zoning Ordinance, Article 23 - 
Signage Ordinance.  
 
9. This Special Use Permit shall run with the land pending compliance with all County, State, 
and Federal requirements. 
 
10. This Special Use Permit shall comply with all Engineering and Flood Control requirements 
prior to issuance of any permits.  
 
11. Any expansion, expiration, or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this 
Permit. 
 
12. If the construction has not begun for the permitted Special Use within a twelve (12) month 
period from the date of the Board approval, this permit shall expire. 
 
13. Any other stipulations that the Planning and Zoning Commission deems necessary.
