Date Received by P&Z:  May 6, 2020  
Re: Questions from the Neighbors 
 
From: Machelle - AJs Getaway RV Park <machelle@ajsgetawayrv.com>  
Sent: Wednesday, May 6, 2020 9:06 AM 
To: Berrin Nejad <berrin.nejad@navajocountyaz.gov>; Cody Cooper <cody.cooper@navajocountyaz.gov>; Bill 
Bess <William.Bess@navajocountyaz.gov>; Sandra Phillips <Sandra.Phillips@navajocountyaz.gov> 
Cc: 'AJ - AJs Getaway RV Park' <aaron@ajsgetawayrv.com>; 'Will Flake' <will.flake@paintedskyes.com>; 
chief@hofdaz.com; 'Haskel Reynolds' <haskelreynolds@yahoo.com> 
Subject: 94 Questions and Answers from Neighbors 
 
 
 
Dear Planning and Zoning, 
 I am sure you have heard of Adrian Bell, our neighbor across the street. He is a local Air BNB owner across Highway 
277 and Buckskin. He also is a Realtor from the Valley with a second home here. We decided to do a friendly 
2nd  Neighborhood meeting as they could not make the first meeting. We had to do a Facebook Live 
meeting/presentation as we couldn’t meet in person. No questions were asked until the next day when these 94 
questions popped up in my email. There are a total of 3 opposing neighbors that are in this group. 
 
It’s unfortunate that he had his friend, Tom Bilsten who i s an apartment/condo builder in the valley and not a 
neighbor or homeowner up here help him come up with all these questions. SO as a good neighbor, I addressed 
them as best as I could. I wanted you to see our conversation before our Zoom Meeting so you are informed of 
this situation in case he speaks up about these questions. Adrian has taken upon himself to speak for the neighbors 
and I am not sure that is even legal as they have never even spoken or emailed me personally. 
 
Thank You P&Z for working so hard with us! 
AJ and Machelle James 
 
 
 
 
 
Adrian and Neighbors, 
Here are your answers as we answered the best way we can as of today. We will not answer any more questions 
at this time. We are very busy parents and we are working behind the scenes to run our business.  
 
Thanks for understanding, 
AJ and Machelle James 
 
 
1.            What is your planned start/completion date for construction?  TBD Depending on approvals and permits. 
2.            Do you plan to construct all of what you showed in the presentation at once, or do you plan to build it out in 
phases? All at once. 
3.            How many trees do you plan to remove?  As few as possible. 
You say you’re not max’ing out the zoned number of RV spaces, but what assurance do we have  
that you will not add more sites later on? We are allowed to expand as we see fit.

<!-- page 284 -->
4.            What is the maximum occupancy of each cabin per night? Occupancy is set by the manufacturer it and 
will be enforced. Typically 4-6 people 
5.            Can you assure us that sewage treatment systems to handle the requirements of the facility NOT have 
any detrimental effect on the surrounding environment, nor the functionality of our existing residential waster 
water treatment facilities? The septic design is determined by the engineers at ADEQ to safely and effectively 
handle our septic system.  It also has to approved by ADEQ for us to  move forward.  
6.            What plans do you and your contractor have to mitigate dust/noise/disruption/damage to Buckskin 
Road caused by construction? Our General Contractor has policies and procedures in place for building and  
keeping dust down with water per N.C. requirements and ROC guidelines. We will also have a water truck on 
premises.  
7.            What is your planned opening date? TBD.  
8.            What are you planned seasonal operating dates — open/closed for season? At this time, we are 
seasonal only. Approx April or May opening to October- November.  Weather permitting.  
How are you planning to ensure adequate and safe removal of all solid waste? Larsen Waste will manage our 
trash removal 
9.            Will you store/sell propane? NO 
10.         Will you sell propane to the public? NO 
11.         If you plan to sell propane where will those tanks be located and how,  
how frequently and where will the replenishment of those tanks occur? NA 
12.         If you plan to store/sell propane, how large will those tanks be, who  
will operate them and will they be above-above-ground or below ground? 
13.         How/where will solid waste dumpsters be placed on the site and how and how often will they be 
emptied? Larsen Waste will decide how often they will be emptied. The trash bins will not be visible from 
Buckskin and behind a block structure 
14.         What is the maximum occupancy of each pull-thru RV space per night? Every RV has sleeping 
Maximums. Our Attorney is working on our private internal campground protocols. 
15.         What is the maximum occupancy of each corral per night? (both in number of RVs as well as people)? 
Our Attorney is working on our private internal campground protocols. 
16.17.    What will the access points be for solid waste truck removal, and will you limit days/hours of such 
removal? We will be on Larsen Waste rotating schedule. We do not know the hours yet. 
18.         Will you allow non-visitor sewage dumping on site? We do not have a dump station here 
19.         What have you done to ensure that storm water drainage caused by the extensive paving throughout 
the site doesn’t create flooding issues on  
Buckskin Road?.  Engineering has performed flood calcs and detention areas which are included in our Submittal 
to Navajo County. 
20.         How do you plan to mitigate dust on Buckskin Road caused by the increased vehicular traffic — both 
RVs, cars and ATVs? Being that this is a heavily used public road already, we will encourage our guests to abide 
by 25 MPH, the posted speed limit. 
21.         How do you plan to manage campers’ speeding on Buckskin Road in any type of vehicle? We have no 
control over other people’s actions as the people on this road will not be just our guests as it is a public road. 
22.         Will you allow registered guests to bring visitors — both day visitors and overnight visitors?  Yes, based 
on maximum occupancy per site. 
23.         Is there a maximum number of day (during 7 a.m. - 10 p.m.) visitors (who are there to visit registered 
guests) allowed per unit? YES. We just don’t know the number yet. Our Attorney is working on this. 
24.         Are day visitors required to check in? YES 
25.         Will the security detail remain on site during their shift? We will not give our Internal Security Measures 
to the Public for our own safety. 
26.         Is there a maximum number of overnight visitors (10 p.m. - 7 a.m.) allowed? YES

<!-- page 285 -->
27.         Are overnight visitors required to check in? ALL Visitors will be required to check in. 
28.         Will you allow tent camping in the corrals, or anywhere on site for that matter? NO 
29.         Will you have an overall total maximum occupancy per night for the whole campground (measured in 
people) and how will you monitor/manage that? No, we can expect anywhere from 1 up to 10 since that is the 
manufacturer recommended occupancy on some of the larger RVs. 
30.         What does “off-road friendly RV park” mean? It means people who have off road vehicles can stay at 
our campground.  Similar to “Horse friendly” or “Kid friendly” 
31.         What are planned hours for the security detail? We will not give our Internal Security Measures to the 
Public for our own safety. 
34.         What will the process be for neighbors to report your guests’ violations  
of any park rules at all times of the day/night?.  We will be in control of our internal campground rules and 
regulations.  
35.         How do you plan to ensure the neighborhood understand the rules you’re holding your guests to? And 
keep us updated as you modify/add new rules for camp guests? We are a private campground and we do not 
need to update the neighbors of our internal rules and security changes. 
36.         You said that security will handle late checkins, who will handle security while security is handling 
administrative needs like late checkins? We will not give our Internal Security Measures to the Public for our 
own safety 
37.         What is the planned height and style of the perimeter fence? 6’ tied together Cedar Fencing except for 
Highway 277 side. That side will be 7ft high , still tied together for privacy. 
38.         What will the store sell?   Incidentals  
39.         What are the planned store hours?  TBD 
40.         Will you rent ATVs?  NO 
41.         Will you rent any other off-road recreational vehicles (including dirt bikes) No 
42.         How will you monitor underage use of off-road vehicles?  Our Attorney & Insurance carrier will advise 
on this. 
43.         How will you mitigate the increased dust caused by your guests’ ATV  
traffic down Buckskin.  It is not our job to police Buckskin Rd. It is also traffic from locals. However, I’m happy to 
support you in placing an anti-dust treatment on Buckskin Rd at your expense.  
44.         How will you work to enforce 25 MPH speed limit on Buckskin Road?    In our signed contract, local laws 
and regulations will be outlined.  However, we cannot control what other people do on public roads. 
45.         Will you provide any other services to non-registered guests on site (RV service/ATV service, for 
example?) NO 
46.         Will the store be open to the public (non-registered guests?) NO 
47.         Will the store sell alcohol? Will you limit alcohol sales during quiet hours? No 
48.         What will you do to ensure no guests park vehicles on Buckskin Road? We will have it written into our 
contract that there is no parking on Buckskin. 
49.         How will delivery trucks access the facility to replenish the store? Will those hours be limited? If so, to 
what hours? We haven’t decided yet as we are not there yet. 
50.         Will you allow generator use? None, unless the power is out in our town. 
51.         What’s your policy for discharging fireworks on the site? We will follow the N.C. fireworks ordinance. 
52.         What’s your policy for discharging firearms/target practice?  We will follow the N.C .firearm laws 
53.         You mentioned that you would allow “late arrivals” - what is your definition of late, and are there cutoff 
times for overnight arrival? After normal business hours, which we have not decided on. 
54.         What’s the rationale for putting the main entrance off Buckskin Road vs. 277? It makes the most sense. 
We are utilizing the existing entrance area that is already here for this property.

<!-- page 286 -->
55.         We would like to see a reoriented site plan where the RV storage (which you indicated is designed to 
serve as a sound buffer) is placed instead facing our homes on Buckskin and the main park entrance is moved to 
277. This option is not available. 
56.         What will your policy be for campers’ visitors? (those not registered to stay as part of a guest 
reservation for a specific camping spot) This will be defined in our internal policies and protocols. They will need 
to check in though. 
57.         Will you allow non-camper/visitor/guest access to the facility for the special events (movies, arts, crafts) 
you mentioned and even other events? Yes 
58.         Will you allow buyouts of the venue for special events like birthdays, weddings, etc?  We haven’t 
decided yet. 
59.         Are you planning to limit the number of ATVs — either per/site, or a maximum overall?  YES 
60.         Have you conducted a Buckskin Road traffic study to determine what additional ATV traffic your guests 
will generate? No as we do not need one per Navajo County. 
61.         Alternatively, have you considered not allowing ATVs to turn right out of the property the same way as 
you’re not allowing RVs to turn right. NO 
62.         During the winter off-season months will those who store their RVs be able to access them/remove 
them?  Yes 
63.         What’s your rationale for the quiet hour window of 10 p.m. - 7 a.m. That’s a respectable time to offer 
our neighbors and our campers for quiet hours as well as an industry standard.  There is NO noise ordinance in 
Navajo County. 
64.         Would you consider an earlier start to quiet hours?  NO 
65.         Will you require camp fires to be extinguished during quiet hours. NO, so long as they are attended to in 
accordance with the fire chief. 
66.         What will you do to police quiet hours, particularly in the corrals. We will have security here to enforce 
our rules. 
67.         What resources will you provide to neighbors to report quiet hour violations and how will you enforce 
them? We will be contacted internally by our security if need be and we will handle it. 
68.         What are you decibel-level limits/criterion for “quiet” hours? We will not be walking around our 
campground with a decibel meter. It will be a reasonable and acceptable amount. 
69.         What’s your policy for noise levels during “non-quiet hours?” — will you allow amplified music during 
non-quiet hours?   There currently is not a noise ordinance in N.C. , however we will control it as needed. 
70.         Will you distributed sound throughout the campground that you control? NO 
71.         Will you allow campers to play amplified music from each RV? At a respectable and non-offensive level 
they can.  Yes 
72.         What sound system are you planning to use for “movie nights” and how does that relate to quiet 
hours?   Our AV tech will recommend what is best. 
73.         How about backup alarms on RVs? Will those be prohibited during quiet hours? (see question about late 
arrivals) We’ve never encountered an RV that has a reverse backup alarm. However with every site being pull 
through, backing up will be at a minimum. 
74.         You mentioned your facility will adhere to a “dark sky” methodology… how will you achieve this dark sky 
objective? what does this mean specific to all lighting throughout the facility?  Low voltage path markers and 
down lighting 
75.         How will you mitigate night sky light pollution while projecting movies on the “movie nights” that you 
mentioned in your presentation?    Movie nights will end prior to quiet hours. 
76.         Will you specify a Kelvin grade for all facility lighting? No as we don’t know the temperature of the 
lighting. All lighting will be low voltage and/or solar. 
77.         Will all facility lights be cowled to prevent light shining upward? Our lights will primarily be downfacing 
when at all possible.

<!-- page 287 -->
78.         Will your planned “water tower signage” be lit at night for visibility from 277? To be determined. 
79.         What will you do to ensure that your campers’ RV lights/portable lights/string lights are restricted to 
ensure skies remain dark? the safety of our guests are top priority.  I will not restrict portable lights that will 
jeopardize their safety. 
80.         What about camp fires? how will you ensure that mulitiple camp fires  
aren’t contributing to nighttime light pollution?  If the fire chief allows open burning of approved materials, we 
will not police it. 
81.         Will you have light pollution hours? NO 
82.         How will you price each unit?  Not public information 
83.         What’s the maximum number of nights/stay?  Not public information at this time 
84.         Will you have a minimum night stay?  Not public information at this time 
85.         What’s the maximum number of additional vehicles allowed per registered RV? including all towed 
vehicles, trailers, dirt bikes, ATVs…  Not public Information at this time as we don’t know yet. 
86.         Will you allow pets?  YES 
87.         Will you have a limit on pets? YES 
88.         Will you have weight limits on pets? Our insurance company will advise. 
89.         Will you have breed restrictions on pets?  This will be based on our Insurance Carrier and we follow their 
instructions. 
90.         How will you ensure pets don’t create issues for safety/noise? They will need to be on leashes and 
attended at all times while in our campground 
91.         Will you allow pet corrals? NO. 
92.         Will you permit animals to be left at sites alone? NO 
93.         Will you have on-site laundry facilities? NO 
94.         Will the laundry facilities be open to guests only? Or to the public? NA

RESOLUTION  02P-20 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
APPROVING A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR AJS GETAWAY RV PARK 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on December 5, 2019  by 
Aaron and Wilma James to construct and operate a 43 RV camp sites and 30 spaces for RV and 
boat storage; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Na vajo County: APN#: 
206-34-003A, T12N, R17E, S20 in Heber area; and 
 
WHEREAS the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on May 21, 2020; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony and other 
evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of  staff, found that the Special 
Use Permit , is consistent with the public health, safety and gener al welfare and should be 
approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors  hereby approves the 
Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions: 
 
 
 
1. That the project is developed in conformance with the development plans submitted for 
the May 21, 2020 Navajo County, Planning and Zoning Commission meeting and may be 
further modified by the Planning and Zoning Commission.  
 
2. That the project conforms with all the comments and the stipulations required by other 
departments and agencies.  All stipulations shall be met before applying for any permit 
and/or issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy.   
 
3. A certificate of zoning compliance documenting the completion of all the stipulations 
shall be issued by the Planning staff prior to the Certificate of Occupancy.  
 
4. All RV park spaces shall have their own pad and utility hookups. 
 
5. All lighting on the property shall face inward, be shielded, be motion-activated, and be in 
conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance.

<!-- page 289 -->
6. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the approved site plan.  
Any expansion or change of the Special Use shall req uire an amendment to this 
SUP approval. 
 
7. Seasonal shut down signs shall be clearly visible from Hwy 260 and shall be in 
accordance  with the Navajo County Sign  ordinance. 
 
8. Any other signage shall be according to the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, 
Article 23- Signage Ordinance.  
 
9. This Special Use Permit shall run with the land pending compliance with all County, 
State, and Federal requirements. 
 
10. This Special Use Permit shall comply with all Engineering and Flood Control 
requirements prior to issuance of any permits.  
 
11. Any expansion, expiration, or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to 
this Permit. 
 
 
12. If the construction has not begun for the permitted Special Use within a 12-month period 
from the date of the Board approval, this permit shall expire. 
 
PASSED AND ADOPTED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas 
and ______ nays on this 23rd day of June 2020.  
 
 
 
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________ 
                                                                            Jason E. Whiting, Chairman of the Board 
ATTEST: 
 
________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

RESOLUTION _____-20 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS DENYING 
A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR AJS GETAWAY RV PARK 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on December 5, 2019  by 
Aaron and Wilma James to construct and operate a 43 RV camp sites and 30 spaces for RV and 
boat storage; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Na vajo County: APN#: 
206-34-003A, T12N, R17E, S20 in Heber area; and 
 
WHEREAS the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on May 21, 2020; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony and other 
evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the reco mmendations of staff, found that the Special 
Use Permit, is not consistent with the public health, safety and gener al welfare and should be 
denied; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors hereby deny the Special 
Use Permit. 
 
DENIED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors by a vote of _____ yeas and ______ nays 
on this 23rd day of June 2020.  
 
 
 
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________ 
                                                                            Jason E. Whiting, Chairman of the Board

________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Chairman Teetsel called the meeting of the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission to order and explained the meeting procedures to the public.   
 
ITEM #1 – PLEDGE OF ALLEGIANCE. 
Mr. Teetsel then elected to postpone the pledge of Allegiance until we could meet in 
person once again, but still gave respects to the flag. 
 
ITEM #2 – REVIEW OF AGENDA BY COMMISSIONERS. 
 
ITEM #3 – CALL TO PUBLIC FOR ITEMS NOT ON THE AGENDA.

<!-- page 292 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                             May 21st, 2020                        Minutes-FINAL 2 
 
ITEM #4 –SUP-A 20-001 HASHKNIFE ENERGY CENTER LLC, DISTRICT III: A request 
for a Special Use Permit Amendment to expand the site area from the previously 
approved 3,000 acres to approximately 3,960 acres, an addition of 960 acres, to 
build and operate a 400 MW solar genera tion and storage facility.  The properties 
are located in the Joseph City area.  APN # 107 -01-023, 107-05-031C, 107-05-031D, 
110-15-001D, & 110-15-008, T18N R19E S30, 31, & 32, T17N, R19E, S05 & 06.  Owner: 
Hashknife Energy Center LLC.  Agent: James Williams. 
 
The item was requested to be tabled until the June 18th meeting. 
 
Chairman called for a motion.  Commissioner Rhodes made a motion to table the item.  
Commissioner Howe seconded the motion.  Motion carried 5 - 0. 
 
ITEM #5 – SUP 20-001 AJ’s RV GETAWAY RV PARK, DISTRICT IV:  A request for a 
Special Use Permit to build and operate a 43 RV-camp sites and 30 spaces RV and 
boat storage. The property is 14.83 acres, addressed as 3537 Buckskin Road and 
located in the Heber area. APN # 20 6-34-003A, T12N R17E S20.  Owner: Aaron and 
Wilma James.  Agent: Painted Sky Engineering & Surveying, LLC. 
 
The Community Development Director Ms. Berrin Nejad introduced the item, noting the 
property’s location in relation to landmarks in the area. The applicant is proposing a short-
term RV park. Surrounding properties are mostly commercial use, with some residential. 
The property was originally three parcels and it has been combined  into one for the 
purposes of the project. The main entry would be from Buckskin Road, and there will be 
emergency exits to the highway.  The flow of the roads are such that there will be no need 
for U-turns within the camp, with some signage for seasonal closures that will be visible 
from the highway, and the entrance will have some area set aside for U-turns just in case 
of accidental turn-ins during the off-season.  
 
There is currently a residence on the property and will be used for the owners as a 
groundkeeper’s quarters. There are plans for a club house and check-in building that will 
have a convenience store, as well as some storage and a workshop area.   There will be 
an area with picnic tables on the west side, the quiet hours will be from 10PM to 7AM. 
 
There will be solid wood fencing all around the property,  and trash will be handled by 
waste management. Trees will be preserved as best they can with plans to plant more, 
and there would be boat and trailer storage alon g the highway. She then shared  some 
renders that showcased the ideal finished project were shared.  
 
The Applicants and their engineer Will Flake were available to answer questions. 
 
Commissioner Howe asked if they were on septic or on the community sewer.  Mr. Flake 
said that they would plan to put in a septic and work with ADEQ on all requirements.  
 
Commissioner DeCross had looked at the property prior, he wanted to know if a nearby 
property with a sign that said “Morgen Stables” was commercial or not. 
 
The Applicant said the zoning of the property in question was A-General.

<!-- page 293 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                             May 21st, 2020                        Minutes-FINAL 3 
 
The Applicants further wanted to thank the commission, but they also wished to note that 
they were going to be providing large spaces, they were conscious of bright lights at night 
and will have downward -facing lamps, they will have nighttime security,  and they were 
working on emergency evacuation plans and they placed the entrance of the campground 
as close to the highway as they could to keep travelers from going too far down buckskin 
road, as well as a crash gate that lets out directly onto the highway. They were not planning 
on permanent residents, and as far as growth goes, they don’t know what the future holds 
but are optimistic and eager to work with Navajo County in the future. 
 
Commissioner DeCross asked how many of the lots were pull -through, the applicant 
replied all the lots were pull-through. 
 
Commissioner Howe asked about the storage units, if they were slips, structures, or if 
they were covered.  Applicant replied that they were slips at a 30-degree angle and would 
not be covered or have any structure over them. 
 
Chairman Teetsel asked about the containers that were to be used as storage, if that was 
in keeping with the code.  The applicant stated they would be set apart with some space 
between that would create the workshop, but they would be painted to beautify the area. 
 
Commissioner DeCross  read a letter from a M r. Adrian Bell, which listed several 
concerns, the largest being the location of the entrance, and wished to know why it could 
not be moved to the highway. 
 
Mr. Flake answered that ingress and egress off of a highway is just a bit more dangerous 
because of the speeds, but noted that there is an existing ingress-egress on the property, 
and altering the entrance would force them to remove well aged trees  they wish to 
preserve in the area for shielding and moving the entrance would force them to cut those 
down.  
 
With no further questions from the commission, the  chairman opened the floor to the 
public. 
 
Adrian Bell noted that his property was just a little north to the entrance of the park as 
proposed, he also listed several neighbors that were close to the project, and stated he 
had reached out and talked to the developers and was just concerned about four things , 
being the access, ATV’s, speeding, dust, and property values. 
 
Nick Deleo voiced much of the same concerns. 
 
Kyle Ritland, who was concerned about the conditions of Buckskin Road and the impact 
the additional traffic would have on it, noted that  Buckskin Road was paved in the 
renderings and wanted to know if that was the intent of the applicant, the chairman noted 
that he would ask that question at the end of public comments. 
 
Dayna and Debbie Young voiced concerns about expansion and approved of the 
seasonal use.   
 
Lynn Legenzoff spoke out in support of the project,  stating that she lived down on the 
end of Buckskin and said that ATV’s already were using Buckskin road to access the forest 
and did not see a reason to consider the project a nuisance.

<!-- page 294 -->
Planning and Zoning Commission                             May 21st, 2020                        Minutes-FINAL 4 
 
Becky McLarty also spoke in support, saying she has  lived in the area for a long time, 
and noting that they don’t really have anything like this in the area, would personally love 
to help support the activities and bring more family -oriented and community culture into 
the area. She further spoke toward the integrity of the applicant. 
 
Sheri Hogg also wished to speak to the integrity of applicant and their desire to do good, 
and spoke about her experiences with the applicant, and give her full support. 
 
Pamela Schwoere also wanted to give her support and speak toward the thor oughness 
the applicants had in researching and working on this project.  
 
Commissioner Howe asked if Buckskin Road was in fact dirt.  The applicant replied that 
it was. Mr. Howe further asked staff what could be done about speeding, and the  dust 
issue as he was sympathetic to it. Mr. Cooper stated that Engineering would be the best 
to address this as Buckskin road is a county-maintained road.  
 
The applicant replied that speeding was a concern, and that their regulations within the 
campground are limiting speed to 25 mph, but felt that that was far too fast, and were 
willing to lower it to 15 if it pleased the commission. 
 
Mr. Bill Bess stated that dust abatements are always a problem during summer, and there 
is not any type of abatement plan  county-wide, but the county is slowly changing road 
materials to help with taking care of the dust  problems.  Commissioner Howe asked 
about the length of Buckskin Road, the applicant said they felt it was about half a mile 
long. 
 
Commissioner Decross agreed and noted that whatever ATV’s or other vehicles  the 
patrons bring in must be street legal or they cannot legally be on Buckskin Road. 
 
Commissioner Howe asked engineering about lowering the speed limit, Mr. Bess replied 
that it’s not safe or conducive and it would require more work- and said that if people aren’t 
obeying the speed limit at 25 mph, they won’t obey 15 mph.  Mr. Bess further said placing 
the entry onto the highway is a very bad and dangerous idea , and ADOT will li kely not 
allow it. Furthermore, the crash gate is emergency only, it’s not there for convenience and 
not considered proper access. 
 
Commissioners Rhoades and DeCross asked if there was type of substance to put on 
the road to keep it from creating dust. Mr.  Bess said there is,  but it’s expensive and the 
county does not have the budget for it. If it’s not kept up it will eventually break apart and 
create potholes, and unsafe driving conditions. 
 
Commissioner Rhoades asked if the applicant could pave the part of the road that goes 
into their entrance, to which Mr. Bess responded that it would make the road unsafe to 
travel upon, and that it was either not paved at all or all paved, mixing materials on a road 
was unwise. 
 
Chairman Teetsel called for a motion.  Commissioner Decross made a motion to 
recommend Approval to the Board.  Commissioner Rhoades seconded the motion.  
Motion carried, 5-0.
